We release all five checkpoints, the training curriculum, and the real-time inference code at https://github.com/OpenMOSS/MOSS-VL.

</details>


### [46] [Frequency and Edge-Guided Segment Anything Model for Remote Sensing Image Semantic Segmentation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.15054)
*Feng Gao,Zizhe Pan,Haoting Wang,Ruzhuang Hua,Jingchao Cao,Junyu Dong,Qian Du*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: FE-SAM is a scalable framework for remote sensing image semantic segmentation that enhances the Segment Anything Model through a Frequency-Modulated Adapter (FMA) for adaptive frequency domain feature decomposition and an EGRefiner for multi-scale edge-enhanced fine-grained detail extraction, outperforming state-of-the-art methods on three benchmarks.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing SAM-based approaches for remote sensing image semantic segmentation suffer from (1) insufficient adaptation of SAM's features to diverse land cover types and (2) semantic ambiguity at object boundaries, both hindering accurate segmentation.

Method: FE-SAM introduces two key components: (1) a Frequency-Modulated Adapter (FMA) that adaptively decomposes and modulates frequency-domain features based on input data, selectively enhancing informative high- and low-frequency components for different land cover types; and (2) EGRefiner, which integrates multi-scale edge-enhanced information from the input image to improve fine-grained detail capture.

Result: Extensive experiments on three benchmark datasets demonstrate that FE-SAM outperforms state-of-the-art methods in remote sensing image semantic segmentation.

Conclusion: The proposed FE-SAM framework effectively addresses the limitations of existing SAM-based approaches by combining frequency-domain feature modulation and edge-guided refinement, providing a scalable and efficient solution for RSISS tasks.

Abstract: Remote sensing image semantic segmentation (RSISS) has attracted significant attention due to the growing demand for fine-grained land cover information. The Segment Anything Model (SAM), proposed as a foundation vision model, offers strong segmentation performance and generalization capabilities for RSISS tasks. However, existing SAM-based approaches face two limitations: (1) Insufficient adaptation of SAM's features to the diverse characteristics of land cover types. (2) Semantic ambiguity at object boundaries, which hinders accurate delineation. To address these limitations, we propose Frequency and Edge-guided SAM (FE-SAM), a scalable and efficient framework for RSISS. Specifically, we introduce a Frequency-Modulated Adapter (FMA) that adaptively decomposes and modulates frequency-domain features based on the input data. It selectively enhances informative high- and low-frequency components corresponding to different land cover types. Furthermore, to improve SAM's ability to capture fine-grained details, we design EGRefiner, which integrates multi-scale edge-enhanced information extracted from the input image. Extensive experiments on three benchmark datasets demonstrate that FE-SAM outperforms state-of-the-art methods. The source codes are available at: https://github.com/oucailab/FE-SAM.

</details>


### [47] [MEDR: Query-Independent Frame Selection via Multi-Signal Event Modeling and Dynamic Rescoring](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.15058)
*Xinlei Pu,Weijie Shi,Wen Yang,Yi Cao,Hao Chen,Yuanjun Liu,Wenwei Ding,Jia Zhu,Jiajie Xu*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: MEDR is a training-free, query-independent frame selection method for multimodal LLMs that uses multi-signal event modeling (combining visual, motion, and text signals) and dynamic rescoring to select informative frames from long videos. The selected frame set is fixed and reusable across different questions, improving accuracy on Video-MME and LongVideoBench by 0.53%-1.23%.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Uniform sampling misses brief informative content, while query-dependent frame selection cannot reuse the same visual input across different questions and requires repeated selection in multi-turn video dialogue. There is a need for a query-independent method that preserves reusability while improving event coverage beyond uniform sampling.

Method: MEDR consists of two components: (1) Multi-Signal Event Modeling, which organizes complementary visual, motion, and text signals into signal-specific temporal events; and (2) Dynamic Rescoring, which iteratively reevaluates each candidate frame relative to the current selected set, updating scores based on frame-level signal strength, additional event coverage, and temporal proximity. The method is training-free and query-independent, producing a fixed frame set reusable across questions.

Result: MEDR improves accuracy by 0.63%-0.89% on Video-MME, by up to 1.23% on the long-video subset of LongVideoBench with Qwen3-VL-8B, and 0.53% overall accuracy improvement while reusing the exact same frame set for every question about a video.

Conclusion: A query-independent, training-free frame selection approach can outperform uniform sampling and maintain reusability of fixed visual inputs, making it practical for multi-turn video dialogue while improving informative event coverage.

Abstract: Frame selection is a fundamental component of multimodal large language models, enabling long videos to be processed under limited visual-token and computational budgets. Uniform sampling preserves temporal coverage but may miss informative content that appears only briefly. To alleviate this limitation, query-dependent methods can retrieve question-relevant frames. However, because the selected frames depend on the current question, the same visual input cannot be directly shared across different questions, and frame selection must be repeated in multi-turn video dialogue. This motivates us to seek a query-independent frame selection method that preserves the reusability of a fixed visual input while improving the coverage of informative events beyond uniform sampling. We propose Multi-Signal Event Modeling and Dynamic Rescoring (MEDR), a training-free and query-independent frame selection method. Multi-Signal Event Modeling organizes complementary visual, motion, and text signals into signal-specific temporal events. Dynamic Rescoring then iteratively reevaluates each candidate relative to the current selected set, updating its score according to frame-level signal strength, additional event coverage, and temporal proximity. The resulting fixed frame set is constructed without observing the query and can be reused across different questions. On the standard benchmark evaluations, MEDR improves model accuracy by 0.63%-0.89% on Video-MME. On the long-video subset of LongVideoBench, it improves accuracy by up to 1.23% with Qwen3-VL-8B. MEDR further improves overall accuracy by 0.53%, while reusing exactly the same frame set for every question about a video.

</details>


### [48] [EgoTac: In-the-wild Tactile Prediction from Egocentric Vision](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.15060)
*Wenkang Zhang,Chengbo Yuan,Zicheng Zhang,Zhengxue Cheng,Yang Gao*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: EgoTac is a generalizable model that predicts tactile information (continuous force measurements and binary contacts) directly from egocentric human videos, trained on 5.7M image-tactile pairs, achieving strong in-domain and out-of-domain performance.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Most egocentric human data used for robot learning lacks tactile information due to sensor limitations in collecting large-scale tactile data, while human video data is abundant and scalable. This raises the question of whether tactile signals can be inferred purely from vision.

Method: EgoTac is trained on a unified corpus of over 5.7M image-tactile pairs covering both continuous force measurements and binary contacts, learning to predict rich tactile information from egocentric human videos.

Result: In-domain prediction achieves average force error below 0.06N. Out-of-domain contact prediction consistently outperforms state-of-the-art contact estimators, captures real tactile data patterns, enables zero-shot predictions on unconstrained real-world videos, and shows steady improvement with data diversity and volume.

Conclusion: EgoTac provides a scalable pathway to extract tactile priors from egocentric human videos, enabling broadly applicable tactile-aware robot learning.

Abstract: Touch is fundamental to dexterous manipulation, yet most egocentric human data increasingly used for robot learning lacks tactile information. Directly collecting large-scale tactile data is challenging due to sensor limitations, while human video data is abundant, contact-rich, and easily scalable. This motivates a natural question: can tactile signals be inferred purely from vision? To address this, we introduce EgoTac, a generalizable model that predicts rich tactile information directly from egocentric human videos. EgoTac is trained on a unified corpus of over 5.7M image-tactile pairs, covering both continuous force measurements and binary contacts. By learning from this diverse dataset, EgoTac captures nuanced touch dynamics across varied interactions. Experiments demonstrate strong performance: in-domain prediction achieves an average force error below 0.06N. On out-of-domain contact prediction benchmarks, EgoTac consistently outperforms the state-of-the-art contact estimator. It also captures the rise and fall patterns of real tactile data and enables zero-shot predictions on unconstrained real-world videos. Scaling analyses further reveal that both data diversity and volume improve performance steadily. Overall, EgoTac provides a scalable pathway to extract tactile priors from egocentric human videos, enabling broadly applicable tactile-aware robot learning.

</details>


### [49] [Do Visual Grounding Decoders Need Feed-Forward Networks? A Controlled Study over Frozen Vision-Language Features](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.15061)
*Tarun Tomar*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: This paper investigates whether feed-forward networks (FFNs) in visual grounding decoders are necessary when using frozen pretrained vision-language model (VLM) features. Comparing attention-only (A4), attention-plus-FFN (S4), and parameter-matched attention-only (A8) decoders, the authors find that attention-only decoders perform comparably to or better than those with FFNs on most benchmarks, with only a small deficit on FineCops-Ref that A8 recovers. Removing FFNs reduces trainable parameters by 44.4% and decoder latency by 10.1%.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To determine whether FFNs in visual grounding decoders provide essential computational value, or whether they are redundant when a pretrained VLM has already encoded image and language context.

Method: The authors compare three decoder architectures over frozen VLM features: (1) A4, a four-block attention-only decoder, (2) S4, a matched four-block attention-plus-FFN decoder, and (3) A8, an eight-block attention-only decoder used as a parameter control to match S4's parameter count. They evaluate on RefCOCOg, Ref-Adv-s, and FineCops-Ref benchmarks, and measure parameter count and latency.

Result: A4 matches or slightly exceeds S4 on RefCOCOg and Ref-Adv-s. On FineCops-Ref, A4 shows a small 0.52 percentage point deficit at IoU@0.5 (95% CI [0.12, 0.95] in favor of S4), but A8 recovers this deficit and finishes 0.26 points above S4. Official FineCops levels do not show a monotonic gap increase. A4 reduces trainable decoder parameters by 44.4% and cached-decoder latency by 10.1%, with end-to-end latency remaining backbone-dominated.

Conclusion: FFNs in visual grounding decoders are largely redundant when operating on frozen VLM features, as attention-only decoders achieve comparable or better performance while being more parameter-efficient and faster. The caveat is that these conclusions apply to the trainable grounding decoder specifically, not to a complete attention-only VLM.

Abstract: Do feed-forward networks (FFNs) in visual grounding decoders add essential computation once a pretrained vision-language model has already encoded image and language context? We compare a four-block attention-only decoder (A4), a matched four-block attention-plus-FFN decoder (S4), and an eight-block attention-only parameter control (A8) over frozen VLM features. A4 matches or slightly exceeds S4 on RefCOCOg and Ref-Adv-s. FineCops-Ref reveals a small A4 deficit of 0.52 percentage points at IoU@0.5 (95% CI [0.12, 0.95] in favor of S4), but A8 recovers it and finishes 0.26 points above S4. Official FineCops levels do not show a monotonic increase in the gap. A4 reduces trainable decoder parameters by 44.4% and cached-decoder latency by 10.1%, although end-to-end latency remains backbone-dominated. These results concern the trainable grounding decoder, not a complete attention-only VLM.

</details>


### [50] [SA-GEM: Scale-Adaptive and Geospatial Evidence-Modulated Token Pruning for Efficient Remote Sensing Large Vision-Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.15075)
*Kexin Ma,Jing Xiao,Bowen Xing,Liang Liao,Chia-Wen Lin*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: SA-GEM is a plug-and-play token pruning framework for remote sensing large vision-language models (RS-LVLMs) that adaptively selects resolution based on query-dependent token granularity and modulates token importance by jointly modeling task relevance, spatial structure, and local redundancy. It demonstrates that higher resolution is not always beneficial and achieves consistent accuracy and efficiency gains over existing pruning methods.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: High-resolution processing in RS-LVLMs causes visual token counts to grow quadratically while important visual evidence is sparse and diluted. Existing token pruning methods use scale-agnostic resolution policies and isolated importance cues, limiting task-aligned granularity adaptation and holistic evidence preservation.

Method: SA-GEM unifies task-adaptive token granularity allocation with holistic geospatial token importance modulation. A lightweight router selects resolution based on query-dependent token granularity, while a token importance modulator jointly models task relevance, spatial structure, and local redundancy to preserve holistic geospatial evidence.

Result: SA-GEM achieves consistent gains in accuracy and efficiency over existing pruning methods. On XLRS-Bench, it surpasses GeoLLaVA-8K by 2.3% in accuracy with a 2.4x total inference speedup.

Conclusion: Once sufficient granularity is reached, token quality matters more than token quantity, suggesting that higher resolution is not universally beneficial for RS-LVLM performance.

Abstract: RS-LVLMs have advanced multimodal understanding of Earth observation imagery, yet their performance is fundamentally constrained by high-resolution processing, as visual token counts grow quadratically with linear input resolution while important visual evidence is inherently sparse and increasingly diluted across the expanded sequence. Existing token pruning methods largely rely on scale-agnostic resolution policies and isolated importance cues, limiting task-aligned granularity adaptation and holistic evidence preservation. To address this, we present Scale-Adaptive and Geospatial Evidence-Modulated Token Pruning (SA-GEM), a plug-and-play framework that unifies task-adaptive token granularity allocation with holistic geospatial token importance modulation. Specifically, a lightweight router selects the resolution based on query-dependent token granularity, while a token importance modulator jointly models task relevance, spatial structure, and local redundancy to preserve holistic geospatial evidence. We show that higher resolution is not universally beneficial and, once sufficient granularity is reached, token quality matters more than token quantity. Experiments across various benchmarks demonstrate that SA-GEM achieves consistent gains in both accuracy and efficiency over existing pruning methods. On XLRS-Bench, it surpasses GeoLLaVA-8K by 2.3% in accuracy with a 2.4 times total inference speedup.

</details>


### [51] [Distribution-free false-alarm calibration and chance-corrected spatial evaluation for industrial anomaly detection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.15090)
*Jie Deng*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: This paper critiques standard metrics (AUROC and raw mask overlap) for industrial visual inspection, which fail to capture false-alarm rates at selected thresholds and can be inflated by recurrent defects and mask geometry. The authors propose combining a distribution-free upper tolerance threshold with a paired-minus-crossed spatial test to compute "spatial-evidence lift" relative to chance overlap. Evaluations on ISP-AD point-defect images and the Magnetic Tile Defect dataset show that most detectors have no significant lift beyond chance, except DINOv2-ASM and patch-memory models (WRN50, ViT-B/16). Sample planning reveals that 150 calibration normals only support claims at false-positive rates ≥1.98%, while 1% requires at least 299 normals. The authors advocate reporting operating-point performance and chance-corrected spatial evidence alongside AUROC and raw overlap.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Standard metrics like AUROC and mask overlap do not specify the false-alarm rate at a chosen threshold, and recurrent defect locations or mask geometry can artificially inflate overlap measurements. This motivates a more rigorous statistical framework for evaluating anomaly detectors' spatial localization performance.

Method: The method combines a distribution-free upper tolerance threshold (for operating-point calibration) with a paired-minus-crossed spatial test. The test compares score-contributing locations against the matched defect mask versus masks from other images; the rate difference defines spatial-evidence lift relative to empirical chance overlap. Exact sample planning establishes required numbers of calibration normals for distribution-free claims at target false-positive rates.

Result: Of 378 alarms across 120 images, 230 overlap the matched mask, yet paired and crossed rates are similar in eight of nine detector-modality cells; only DINOv2-ASM shows a positive 95% bootstrap lower bound (lift 0.259). On Magnetic Tile Defect data, lifts are 0.203 (WRN50) and 0.231 (ViT-B/16) with p=1e-5, remaining significant (0.185 and 0.210) under same-class crossed masks. Sample planning shows 150 normals suffice only for target FPR ≥1.98%; 1% requires ≥299 normals.

Conclusion: The findings support reporting operating-point performance and chance-corrected spatial evidence alongside AUROC and raw mask overlap, as raw overlap is often inflated by chance alignment with recurring defect locations and mask geometry.

Abstract: Studies of industrial visual inspection commonly report the area under the receiver operating characteristic curve (AUROC) and the overlap between anomaly maps and defect masks. Neither measure specifies the false-alarm rate at a selected threshold, while recurrent defect locations and mask geometry can inflate overlap. We combine a distribution-free upper tolerance threshold with a paired-minus-crossed spatial test. This test compares each detector's score-contributing locations with the matched defect mask and with masks from other images; the difference in rates defines spatial-evidence lift relative to the empirical chance-overlap rate. We evaluate three detectors on 120 point-defect images from three ISP-AD modalities and three fixed data splits. Of 378 alarms, 230 overlap the matched mask. Paired and crossed rates are nevertheless similar in eight of nine detector--modality cells; only DINOv2--ASM has a positive 95\% bootstrap lower bound (lift 0.259, 95\% interval 0.159--0.347). On the independent Magnetic Tile Defect dataset, the same analysis gives lifts of 0.203 (0.169--0.236) for Wide ResNet-50 (WRN50) patch memory and 0.231 (0.202--0.262) for Vision Transformer B/16 (ViT-B/16) patch memory, with one-sided permutation $p=10^{-5}$ for both. When crossed masks are restricted to the same defect class, the lifts remain 0.185 and 0.210. Exact sample planning shows that, with 150 calibration normals, a 95\%-confidence distribution-free claim is supported only for target false-positive rates of 1.98\% or higher; a 1\% target requires at least 299 normals. The results support reporting operating-point performance and chance-corrected spatial evidence alongside AUROC and raw mask overlap.

</details>


### [52] [MODAL: Multi-Modal Object Re-ID via Model-Driven Sparse Decoupling and Text-Image Differential Filtering](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.15096)
*Chengbo Huang,Jun-Jie Huang,Long Lan,Tianrui Liu,Xueqiong Li,Yuanxi Peng,Xinwang Liu,Meng Wang*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: MODAL is a new multi-modal object re-identification framework that uses coupled sparse coding theory and differential suppression to explicitly disentangle multi-modal features into uni-modal, bi-modal, and tri-modal shared representations, enabling robust performance under modality-missing conditions and state-of-the-art results on four datasets.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing multi-modal Re-ID methods lack principled feature disentanglement and coherent multi-modal integration, leading to entangled representations that cause cross-modal conflicts, obscure discriminative cues, and suffer distribution shift when modalities are missing.

Method: MODAL consists of two core modules: (1) a Multi-modal Feature Sparse Decoupling module built on model-driven deep unrolling with multi-modal coupled sparse coding, which explicitly decomposes features into uni-modal specific, bi-modal, and tri-modal shared representations; and (2) a Text-Image Differential Filtering module that uses coarse-grained textual semantics to adaptively suppress task-irrelevant visual responses. A Modality-Aware Subspace Activation mechanism selectively activates only consistently shared subspaces to handle incomplete-modality scenarios.

Result: Extensive experiments on four datasets demonstrate that MODAL achieves state-of-the-art performance with superior transparency.

Conclusion: The principled feature disentanglement and coherent multi-modal integration in MODAL effectively mitigate cross-modal conflicts and modality-missing degradation, establishing a more transparent and robust foundation for multi-modal object re-identification.

Abstract: Multi-modal object re-identification (Re-ID) aims to facilitate cross-camera object retrieval in complex environments by leveraging complementary information from visual (e.g., RGB, NIR, TIR) and textual modalities. However, existing approaches often lack principled feature disentanglement and coherent multi-modal integration, leading to entangled representations that introduce cross-modal conflicts, obscure discriminative cues, and suffer distribution shift under modality-missing conditions. To tackle these challenges, we propose MODAL, a novel multi-modal object re-identification framework, grounded in coupled sparse coding theory and differential suppression principles. A core component of MODAL is a Multi-modal Feature Sparse Decoupling module, developed in a model-driven deep unrolling manner based on multi-modal coupled sparse coding. It explicitly decomposes multi-modal features into uni-modal specific, bi-modal and tri-modal shared representations, thereby achieving more transparent and effective feature disentanglement. Benefiting from the principled feature disentanglement, MODAL naturally mitigates performance degradation in incomplete-modality scenarios via a Modality-Aware Subspace Activation that selectively activates only the consistently shared subspaces. Moreover, we propose a Text-Image Differential Filtering module that leverages coarse-grained textual semantics to adaptively suppress task-irrelevant responses in the decoupled visual representations, thereby enhancing discriminative information. Extensive experiments on four datasets demonstrate that MODAL achieves state-of-the-art performance with superior transparency.

</details>


### [53] [ProjFormer: Point Cloud Completion via Geometric-Projective Transformer and Cross-Modal Semantic Constraints](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.15104)
*Sheng Liu,Meng Wang,Ruihui Li,Huilong Pi,Zhuo Tang,Kenli Li*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: ProjFormer is a cross-modal point cloud completion framework that uses explicit projection-based alignment and adaptive feature routing to achieve geometry-consistent 2D-3D interaction, delivering competitive performance with improved structural completeness under a lightweight design.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Point cloud completion is ill-posed due to sparsity and ambiguity. Existing multi-view methods incorporate 2D semantics but rely on learned attention and fixed fusion that lack geometric consistency and adaptability.

Method: ProjFormer proposes a cross-modal framework with two key components: (1) a Projective Guided View Attention module that aligns 3D points with multi-view features via deterministic projection for geometrically consistent aggregation, and (2) a geometry-aware routing network that performs point-wise adaptive fusion of structural and observation-driven features for progressive refinement.

Result: Under a lightweight design, ProjFormer delivers competitive performance with improved structural completeness compared to existing methods.

Conclusion: Explicit projection and adaptive feature routing provide better geometric consistency and adaptability than learned attention and fixed fusion in multi-view point cloud completion.

Abstract: Point cloud completion is inherently ill-posed due to severe sparsity and ambiguity in partial observations. Existing multi-view methods alleviate this by incorporating 2D semantics, but often rely on learned attention and fixed fusion, which lack geometric consistency and adaptability. We propose ProjFormer, a cross-modal framework that enforces geometry-consistent 2D-3D interaction through explicit projection and adaptive feature routing. A Projective Guided View Attention module aligns 3D points with multi-view features via deterministic projection, enabling efficient and geometrically consistent aggregation. Building on this, a geometry-aware routing network performs point-wise adaptive fusion of structural and observation-driven features for progressive refinement. Experiments show that, under a lightweight design, ProjFormer delivers competitive performance with improved structural completeness.

</details>


### [54] [CETalk: Continuous Valence-Arousal Control for Audio-Driven 3D Talking Head Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.15110)
*Peng Jia,Li Dai,Zhen Xiao,Xueliang Liu,Jia Li*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: CETalk is an audio-driven 3D facial animation framework that uses continuous Valence-Arousal (VA) representations for fine-grained emotional control, addressing limitations of discrete emotion categories and temporal frequency mismatches in existing methods.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing emotional 3D talking head methods rely on discrete emotion categories, which fail to capture continuous affect evolution, and overlook the temporal frequency mismatch between fast audio articulation and slower emotional expression.

Method: CETalk predicts FLAME parameters via three components: (1) a Dynamic Emotion Modulation Module that adaptively scales emotional intensity from audio cues; (2) a Multi-Scale Temporal Modeling mechanism with parallel branches decoupling high-frequency articulatory movements from low-frequency emotional dynamics; and (3) a Dynamic Fusion Mechanism integrating multi-scale features through an adaptive gating network. A new dataset, 3D-VA-MEAD, is constructed with VA annotations and reconstructed 3D facial motions.

Result: CETalk outperforms state-of-the-art methods in both lip-sync accuracy and emotional expressiveness, while enabling smooth and controllable emotion transitions.

Conclusion: Continuous VA-based emotion conditioning with multi-scale temporal modeling is an effective approach for emotional 3D talking head generation, offering finer-grained control and smooth emotion transitions compared to discrete-category methods.

Abstract: Emotional 3D talking head generation aims to synthesize expressive facial animations with accurate lip synchronization. However, existing methods often rely on discrete emotion categories, which fail to capture the continuous evolution of affect. They also overlook the temporal frequency mismatch between audio articulation and emotional expression. In this paper, we propose CETalk, an audio-driven 3D facial animation framework conditioned on continuous Valence--Arousal (VA) representations for fine-grained emotion control. CETalk predicts a sequence of FLAME parameters through three key components: a Dynamic Emotion Modulation Module that adaptively scales emotional intensity using audio-derived cues; a Multi-Scale Temporal Modeling mechanism that employs parallel branches to decouple high-frequency articulatory movements from low-frequency emotional dynamics; and a Dynamic Fusion Mechanism that integrates these multi-scale features via an adaptive gating network. To support training and evaluation, we construct 3D-VA-MEAD, a large-scale dataset with automatically estimated VA annotations and reconstructed 3D facial motions. Extensive experiments demonstrate that CETalk outperforms state-of-the-art methods in both lip-sync accuracy and emotional expressiveness, while enabling smooth and controllable emotion transitions.

</details>


### [55] [Fast Test-Time Refinement for Robust Learned Image Compression](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.15113)
*Jiaming Liang,Chi-Man Pun,Weisi Lin*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: This paper systematically studies the adversarial vulnerability of Learned Image Compression (LIC) systems and proposes a Fast Test-Time Refinement (FTTR) framework for robust and practical LIC systems, based on a discovered Asymmetric Adversarial Trajectory property and a two-dimensional Tube Model explanation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: LIC systems show strong rate-distortion performance in benign settings but are vulnerable to adversarial attacks due to their high representational capacity. Existing test-time refinement (TTR) defense methods are computationally expensive, lack theoretical grounding, and have not been evaluated in white-box settings or against diverse attack types beyond l2-bounded untargeted distortion attacks.

Method: The paper discovers an Asymmetric Adversarial Trajectory (AAT) property in LIC systems, where moving from adversarial to benign regions is much easier than the reverse. It proposes a two-dimensional Tube Model to explain this phenomenon and introduces a Fast Test-Time Refinement (FTTR) framework built upon the AAT property, which requires only 1-2 steps to recover adversarial examples.

Result: Extensive evaluations with diverse strong adaptive attacks across multiple LIC systems demonstrate the effectiveness and promise of the proposed FTTR framework. The robustness is shown to stem from the contraction of adversarial regions induced by the Input-as-Label property of LIC systems, not from obfuscated gradients.

Conclusion: The FTTR framework provides a computationally efficient (fast) and theoretically grounded defense mechanism for LIC systems, addressing the gap in adversarial robustness evaluation for learned image compression codecs.

Abstract: Learned image compression (LIC) has demonstrated remarkable rate-distortion (RD) performance in benign settings. However, the high representational capacity endowed by deep neural networks (DNNs) comes at the expense of increased adversarial vulnerability. This hinders their adoption as trusted standardized codecs. Recent work has sketched test-time refinement (TTR) as a defense in gray-box scenarios, despite its original purpose of improving benign RD performance. Unfortunately, extensive iterations of TTR incur prohibitive overhead, while the robustness mechanism lacks theoretical understanding. Moreover, TTR has not been evaluated in white-box settings or against attacks beyond $\ell_2$-bounded rate and untargeted distortion objectives. To bridge these gaps, we present a systematic study. Our study reveals an Asymmetric Adversarial Trajectory (AAT) property in LIC systems: transitioning from adversarial to benign regions is significantly easier than the reverse process, where adversarial examples can often be roughly recovered within only 1-2 steps. We provide a two-dimensional Tube Model to explain this phenomenon. Based on AAT, we propose a Fast Test-Time Refinement (FTTR) framework for practical and robust LIC systems. We establish that the robustness arises from the contraction of adversarial regions induced by the Input-as-Label property of LIC systems, rather than from obfuscated gradients. Extensive evaluations with diverse strong adaptive attacks across multiple LIC systems demonstrate the promise of the proposed FTTR framework. The code is available at https://github.com/chinaliangjiaming/FTTR.git.

</details>


### [56] [Perspective-Invariant Attack with Enhanced Transferability of Adversarial Examples](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.15115)
*Kaisheng Liang,Yiming Cao,Bin Xiao*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: The paper proposes PIA (Perspective-Invariant Attack), which enhances adversarial transferability by using multi-DOF vertex sampling to generate perspective-transformed input variations spanning from 2-DOF translation to 8-DOF projective mapping, along with PIA-Mix, a generic extension combining perspective transformations with auxiliary methods.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing input transformation techniques for improving adversarial transferability primarily rely on local operations with limited degrees of freedom (e.g., block shuffling, resizing), overlooking global perspective transformations that naturally arise from viewpoint changes, which limits their effectiveness.

Method: PIA introduces a multi-DOF vertex sampling strategy that systematically covers the perspective transformation hierarchy from 2-DOF translation to 8-DOF projective mapping, generating geometrically diverse input variations to reduce overfitting of adversarial perturbations to the surrogate model. PIA-Mix maintains a complementary transformation pool and efficiently combines perspective transformation with auxiliary methods.

Result: Extensive experiments across various DNN architectures, advanced defense mechanisms, and multimodal large language models (LLMs) demonstrate that PIA and PIA-Mix outperform state-of-the-art transfer-based attacks.

Conclusion: Perspective-invariant transformations effectively enhance adversarial transferability, and PIA-Mix provides a flexible framework for combining these with existing methods, offering superior attack performance across diverse models and defense scenarios.

Abstract: Adversarial examples generated on a surrogate deep neural network (DNN) can often successfully fool other black-box DNN models. This cross-model transferability poses serious security threats to DNNs in practical applications. Input transformation techniques are widely used to enhance adversarial transferability by increasing the diversity of input images. However, existing methods primarily rely on local operations with limited degrees of freedom (DOF), such as block-wise shuffling and resizing, overlooking global perspective transformations that naturally arise from viewpoint changes. In this work, we propose a Perspective-Invariant Attack (PIA), which introduces a multi-DOF vertex sampling strategy that systematically covers the perspective transformation hierarchy from 2-DOF translation to 8-DOF projective mapping. By generating geometrically diverse input variations, PIA effectively reduces overfitting of adversarial perturbations to the surrogate model, thereby improving adversarial transferability. We further propose PIA-Mix, a generic extension that maintains a complementary transformation pool and efficiently combines our perspective transformation with auxiliary methods for improved transferability. Extensive experiments involving various DNN architectures, advanced defense mechanisms, and multimodal large language models (LLMs) demonstrate that PIA and PIA-Mix outperform state-of-the-art transfer-based attacks.

</details>


### [57] [HOIMask: Towards Generative Masked Modeling for Human Object Interaction Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.15141)
*Yihong Ji,Jinsong Zhang,He Hu,Hongbo Xu*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: HOIMask is the first generative masked framework for human-object interaction (HOI) motion modeling in discrete space, using HOI Vector Quantization to encode motion and contact signals into 2D token maps, a transformer-based masked modeling framework, and contact-aware reconstruction guidance to outperform diffusion-based methods.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Diffusion-based HOI generation methods dominate but suffer from high artifacts and unstable interaction quality due to error accumulation during iterative denoising.

Method: HOIMask encodes motion sequences and contact-aware signals into discrete 2D human and object token maps via HOI Vector Quantization, employs a generative masked modeling framework with a transformer architecture to capture spatial-temporal and interaction dependencies, and introduces contact-aware reconstruction guidance during inference to optimize HOI tokens for improved spatio-temporal consistency.

Result: HOIMask outperforms state-of-the-art diffusion-based methods, generating more realistic and semantically aligned HOI motions.

Conclusion: The combination of motion interaction tokens, a dedicated transformer architecture, and the contact-aware guidance strategy enables superior HOI motion generation over diffusion-based baselines.

Abstract: Diffusion-based methods have dominated the HOI generation, as they enable critical contact fusions or signals to guide the diffusion process. However, they often result in high artifacts and unstable interaction quality due to error accumulation during iterative denoising. In this work, we propose HOIMask, the first generative masked framework for modeling HOI motion in discrete space. HOIMask first encodes both motion sequences and contact-aware signals into discrete 2D human and object token maps via HOI Vector Quantization (VQ), preserving fine-grained spatial-temporal structure beyond conventional 1D representations. On this basis, a generative masked modeling framework is employed to jointly capture human-object interaction dynamics, leveraging a transformer architecture designed to model complex spatial-temporal and interaction dependencies. To generate more coherent and physically plausible motions, we further introduce a novel contact-aware reconstruction guidance in discrete space during inference, which fuses contact signals to optimize HOI tokens that forces the generated motion with higher spatio-temporal consistency. With craftily designed motion interaction tokens, dedicated architecture and guidance strategy, HOIMask outperforms state-of-the-art diffusion-based methods, generating more realistic and semantically aligned HOI motions. Please refer to https://jyhflash.github.io/HOIMask/ for more results.

</details>


### [58] [A Unified Backbone--Expert Framework with Relation-Token and Residual--Classifier Interfaces for Automatic Modulation Recognition](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.15160)
*Zhixiang Deng,Houbiao Li,Zongyong Cui*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: This paper proposes a unified backbone-expert framework for automatic modulation recognition (AMR) that addresses representation bottlenecks under varying observation lengths. It uses a common convolutional state-space backbone with two specialized interfaces: lag-aware complex-plane descriptors for short sequences and a gated multi-scale residual refinement module with fixed-averaging classifier collaboration for long sequences. The framework achieves 67.28±0.14% on RML2016.10b and 87.19±0.77% on HisarMod2019.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: A single model architecture often fails to perform well across varying observation lengths in AMR, facing distinct representation bottlenecks depending on sequence length.

Method: A unified backbone-expert framework consisting of a common convolutional state-space backbone with two specialized interfaces. For short sequences, explicit lag-aware complex-plane descriptors are injected as relation tokens. For long sequences, a gated multi-scale residual refinement module corrects feature maps, combined with a fixed-averaging classifier collaboration.

Result: Achieves 67.28±0.14% average accuracy on RML2016.10b and 87.19±0.77% on HisarMod2019 (mean±std over three runs). Validated through three-seed ablations, native-length cross-configuration tests, and controlled window studies.

Conclusion: The expert-interface decoupling approach is confirmed to be more beneficial than one-size-fits-all architectures for AMR under varying observation lengths.

Abstract: Automatic modulation recognition (AMR) faces distinct representation bottlenecks under varying observation lengths, where a single model architecture often fails to excel. To address this, we propose a unified backbone-expert framework with a common convolutional state-space backbone and two specialized interfaces. For short sequences, we inject explicit lag-aware complex-plane descriptors as relation tokens before encoding to compensate for information loss. For long sequences, we design a gated multi-scale residual refinement module to correct the feature map, combined with a fixed-averaging classifier collaboration to harness complementary evidence. Our framework achieves overall average accuracies of 67.28 \pm 0.14% on RML2016.10b and 87.19 \pm 0.77% on HisarMod2019 (mean \pm sample standard deviation over three runs), respectively. The framework's efficacy is further validated through three-seed ablations, native-length cross-configuration tests, and controlled window studies, confirming the benefit of expert-interface decoupling over one-size-fits-all architectures.

</details>


### [59] [From "What-If" to "What-Is": Counterfactual Thinking-Inspired Semantic Alignment for Visual Brain Decoding](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.15163)
*Kaitao Yan,Chi Liu,Congcong Zhu,Huajie Chen,Gengshen Wu,Minghao Wang,Xiaotong Han,Tianqing Zhu*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: ConceptAlign is a counterfactual semantic alignment framework for visual brain decoding that aligns decoded fMRI-derived visual tokens with ground-truth captions in a frozen text-embedding space while separating them from LLM-generated scene-preserving near-miss alternatives, improving semantic fidelity and fine-grained discrimination.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Visual brain decoding reconstructions can be visually plausible yet semantically wrong (incorrect objects, attributes, or relations) because strong generative priors fill in content not sufficiently specified by the decoded representation. Conventional reconstruction metrics assess only the final image, obscuring these semantic errors.

Method: ConceptAlign pools decoded visual tokens and projects them into a frozen text-embedding space, aligning them with the ground-truth caption while separating them from counterfactual near-miss alternatives (generated offline by an LLM) that modify one critical object, attribute, or relation while preserving the scene. A margin-based objective learns fine-grained semantic boundaries, with no LLM calls needed at inference.

Result: On the Natural Scenes Dataset, ConceptAlign improves reconstruction measures, counterfactual semantic discrimination, and representational alignment over the MindEye2 backbone. Matched negative-source ablations, independent LLM/human-written alternatives, and human evaluation support effectiveness and robustness, with favorable patterns in fine-grained conflicts, limited-data decoding, and cross-subject structure.

Conclusion: ConceptAlign demonstrates that counterfactual semantic alignment supervision improves the semantic fidelity of visual brain decoding beyond what perceptual reconstruction metrics capture, and introduces a systematic three-level semantic evaluation framework for assessing decoding quality.

Abstract: Visual brain decoding reconstructs visual content perceived by a person from neural measurements such as fMRI, providing a computational approach to studying how visual information is represented in the brain. Recent multimodal representations and diffusion priors have improved reconstruction realism. However, visually plausible reconstructions may contain incorrect objects, attributes, or relations because a strong generative prior can complete content not sufficiently specified by the decoded representation. Conventional reconstruction metrics mainly assess the final image and may therefore obscure such semantic errors. We propose ConceptAlign, a counterfactual semantic alignment framework for visual brain decoding. ConceptAlign pools decoded visual tokens and projects them into a frozen text-embedding space, aligning the representation with the ground-truth caption while separating it from scene-preserving near-miss alternatives. Generated offline by an LLM, these alternatives modify one critical object, attribute, or relation while retaining the scene. A margin-based objective learns fine-grained semantic boundaries between the observed stimulus and plausible but incorrect interpretations without requiring LLM calls during inference. We introduce a systematic three-level semantic evaluation framework covering foundational discriminability, counterfactual description discrimination, and representational geometry. Experiments on the Natural Scenes Dataset show that ConceptAlign improves reconstruction measures, counterfactual semantic discrimination, and representational alignment over the MindEye2 backbone. Matched negative-source ablations, independent LLM and human-written alternatives, and human evaluation support the effectiveness and robustness of the supervision, with favorable patterns in fine-grained conflicts, limited-data decoding, and cross-subject structure.

</details>


### [60] [Beyond Natural-Image Foundation Models: Benchmarking Satellite Pretraining for Ophthalmic Image Analysis](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.15195)
*Lovre Antonio Budimir,Mingya Alexa Gong,Alyssa Foong Quinney,Ivana Matovinović,Yukun Zhou,Pearse A. Keane,Sven Lončarić,Marinko V. Šarunić*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: This paper proposes using satellite imagery as a novel pretraining domain for medical Vision Foundation Models (MedVFMs), showing that satellite-pretrained DINOv3 (493M images) outperforms natural-image pretraining (1.7B images) on ophthalmic tasks, and in some cases matches or exceeds medical specialist models without using any medical data.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Medical VFMs face constraints from limited data availability, privacy concerns, and high development costs. Since MedVFMs typically rely on weights pretrained on natural images, they suffer from a significant distribution shift when adapted to medical tasks. The authors propose satellite imagery as an alternative pretraining domain because it is visually more aligned with medical data and free from privacy constraints restricting medical datasets.

Method: The authors compare DINOv3 models pretrained on 493 million satellite images (DINOv3-SAT493m) against DINOv3 pretrained on 1.7 billion natural images (DINOv3-LVD1689m), as well as two medical specialist baselines (DINOv3-RETFound and MAE-RETFound). Evaluations are conducted across multiple ophthalmic imaging modalities, with particular focus on en face vascular-rich imagery.

Result: Satellite imagery proves to be a stronger pretraining source than natural images for ophthalmic tasks, especially on en face vascular-rich modalities. On several tasks, satellite pretraining matches or exceeds the medical specialist baselines on high-resolution en face inputs, despite using no medical data during pretraining.

Conclusion: Satellite imagery offers a promising, privacy-free pretraining alternative for MedVFM development and benchmarking, potentially reducing the reliance on natural image pretraining and addressing the distribution shift problem in medical image analysis.

Abstract: Vision Foundation Models (VFMs) have emerged as a promising approach in medical imaging, producing broadly applicable systems that can be efficiently adapted across diverse imaging modalities, anatomical regions, and clinical tasks. However, VFMs require extensive training data, and their progress in medical image analysis is constrained by limited data availability, privacy concerns, and high development costs. To alleviate these constraints, medical VFMs (MedVFMs) are often built upon weights from generalist models pretrained on vast amounts of publicly available natural images, introducing a substantial distribution shift for medical task adaptation. To address this, we propose satellite imagery as a novel pretraining domain for MedVFM development and benchmarking, motivated by its closer visual alignment with medical data and its freedom from the privacy constraints that limit medical datasets. Across multiple ophthalmic imaging modalities, we compare DINOv3-SAT493m pretrained on 493 million satellite images against DINOv3-LVD1689m pretrained on 1.7 billion natural images, together with two medical specialist baselines: DINOv3-RETFound and MAE-RETFound. Our experiments show that satellite imagery is a stronger pretraining source than natural images for ophthalmic tasks, particularly on en face vascular-rich modalities. On several tasks, satellite pretraining matches or exceeds the medical specialists on high-resolution en face inputs, despite using no medical data.

</details>


### [61] [Anchor-Regularized Adaptation for Generalizable AI-Generated Image Detection with DINOv3](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.15196)
*Hyeongjun Choi,Juhun Lee,Davide Cozzolino,Luisa Verdoliva,Simon S. Woo*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: The paper proposes Anchor-Regularized Adaptation (ARA), a method for AI-generated image detection that combines Low-Rank Adaptation to capture pixel-level artifacts with a frozen anchor classifier to preserve the original representation structure. This approach enables complementary supervision from aligned and misaligned training data, achieving state-of-the-art performance on nine benchmarks.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: While aligned training data improves generalization in AI-generated image detection by removing spurious correlations, frozen DINOv3 representations surprisingly perform well even with misaligned training. Naively mixing aligned and misaligned data improves sensitivity to pixel artifacts but distorts the pre-trained representation, limiting generalization. The authors aim to exploit both data types without sacrificing representation fidelity.

Method: The authors first analyze why frozen DINOv3 representations generalize well (reliance on faithful features of authentic images) and where they fall short (capturing subtle pixel-artifact cues). They then propose Anchor-Regularized Adaptation (ARA), which applies Low-Rank Adaptation (LoRA) to capture pixel-level artifacts while using a frozen anchor classifier to prevent deviations from the original representation structure, allowing complementary use of aligned and misaligned data.

Result: ARA achieves state-of-the-art performance on nine diverse and challenging benchmarks, demonstrating that it enables the model to exploit pixel-artifact cues from misaligned data while preserving the generalization benefits of aligned training.

Conclusion: Complementary supervision from misaligned and aligned data can be effectively combined via anchor regularization, yielding superior AI-generated image detection performance without sacrificing generalization.

Abstract: Recent works in AI-generated image detection have shown that careful training data alignment can improve generalization by removing spurious correlations. However, linear probes on frozen DINOv3 representations achieve remarkably strong performance even when trained on misaligned datasets. Motivated by this result, we analyze the underlying rationale and the limits of this generalization. We find that frozen DINOv3 performs well because its decisions rely on features that faithfully represent the space of authentic images. At the same time, its final layer is less effective at capturing the subtle pixel-artifact cues that can be emphasized by aligned training data. We further observe that naively mixing aligned and misaligned data during adaptation improves sensitivity to such cues but at the cost of distorting the pre-trained representation, limiting generalization. To address this issue, we propose Anchor-Regularized Adaptation (ARA). We apply Low-Rank Adaptation to capture pixel-level artifacts while leveraging a frozen anchor classifier to avoid deviations from the original representation structure. This allows the model to exploit pixel-artifact cues without sacrificing generalization. Our method achieves state-of-the-art performance on nine diverse and challenging benchmarks, indicating that ARA enables complementary supervision from misaligned and aligned data for more effective detection.

</details>


### [62] [TERRA: A Hierarchical Parallel Training and Memory Orchestration Framework for High-Resolution AI-based Earth Modeling](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.15211)
*Ruohan Wu,Ziqi Zhu,Yang Zhao,Jiarui Tang,Yingzhe Cui,Junshi Chen,Zhao Jing,Jun Shi,Hong An*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: TERRA is a hierarchical parallel training framework for high-resolution AI-based Earth forecasting models, introducing Sampling-Aware Window, Sequence, and Tensor Parallelism (SAWSTP) and Memory Orchestration (MO) to enable training models up to 11.4B parameters on 96 H200 GPUs with up to 39.76 PFLOPS, achieving 65.0% strong-scaling and 94.1% weak-scaling efficiency while reducing peak GPU memory by 32.2%-51.8% for long-lead finetuning.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Training high-resolution Earth forecasting models is memory-intensive. Existing distributed systems like AERIS focus on pixel-level models and do not jointly support convolutional sampling modules and shifted-window execution. Long-lead rollout finetuning further increases activation memory, making it infeasible to train large models effectively.

Method: TERRA introduces two key components: (1) SAWSTP (Sampling-Aware Window, Sequence, and Tensor Parallelism) that preserves spatially contiguous layouts for convolutional sampling modules while routing tokens into topology-aware ragged window layouts for Swin Transformer execution, and (2) Memory Orchestration (MO) for long-lead finetuning that provides rollout-aware checkpoint planning combined with input buffering and budget-constrained activation offloading.

Result: On the 1/12-degree GLORYS-based Wenhai workload, TERRA supports up to 11.4B parameter models on 96 H200 GPUs, sustains up to 39.76 PFLOPS, achieves 65.0% strong-scaling and 94.1% weak-scaling efficiency. MO reduces peak GPU memory by 32.2%-51.8% compared to checkpoint-only policies with at most 20.0% step-time overhead, enabling finetuning with smaller patch sizes and longer rollouts.

Conclusion: TERRA makes large-scale, high-resolution Earth forecasting model training feasible and efficient through its hierarchical parallel training framework, specifically addressing the challenges of shifted-window Transformers combined with sampling modules and memory-intensive long-lead finetuning, ultimately enabling improved forecasting accuracy through more flexible finetuning configurations.

Abstract: Training high-resolution AI-based Earth forecasting models is memory-intensive. Window-based Swin Transformers reduce the quadratic cost of global attention, but existing distributed systems such as AERIS primarily target pixel-level models and do not jointly support convolutional sampling modules and shifted-window execution. Long-lead rollout finetuning further increases activation memory. To address these challenges, we present TERRA, a hierarchical parallel training framework for high-resolution Earth forecasting. TERRA introduces Sampling-Aware Window, Sequence, and Tensor Parallelism (SAWSTP), which preserves spatially contiguous layouts for sampling modules and routes tokens into topology-aware ragged window layouts for Transformer execution. For long-lead finetuning, Memory Orchestration (MO) provides rollout-aware checkpoint planning and combines input buffering with budget-constrained activation offloading. Experiments on the $1/12^\circ$ GLORYS-based Wenhai workload show that TERRA supports models with up to 11.4B parameters on 96 H200 GPUs and sustains up to $39.76$ PFLOPS, achieving $65.0\%$ strong-scaling and $94.1\%$ weak-scaling efficiency. Compared with checkpoint-only policies, MO further reduces peak allocated GPU memory by $32.2\%$--$51.8\%$ with at most $20.0\%$ step-time overhead, which makes finetuning with smaller patch sizes and longer rollouts feasible for improved forecasting accuracy.

</details>


### [63] [DCA-MoE: Spatially Adaptive Cross-Layer Fusion and Density-Routed Experts for Crowd Counting](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.15213)
*Hao Wang*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: DCA-MoE is a crowd counting framework using a frozen DINOv3 encoder with two content-dependent components: Spatially Adaptive Layer Fusion (SALF) for position-wise feature weighting and Density-Routed Multi-Receptive-Field Experts (DR-MoE) for per-location soft mixtures of experts with different receptive fields. It achieves 31.7 MAE/72.2 RMSE on NWPU-Crowd validation with ViT-L/16, but cross-dataset results are mixed and causal attribution requires more paired multi-seed evaluation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Crowd counting suffers from severe variations in perspective, head scale, occlusion, and background clutter. Existing multi-level decoders use spatially invariant feature fusion and a single receptive-field pattern for all locations, which is suboptimal for handling these heterogeneous spatial conditions.

Method: The framework freezes a DINOv3 encoder and introduces two content-dependent components: (1) SALF predicts position-wise weights over four aligned backbone features for spatially adaptive fusion, and (2) DR-MoE assigns each location a soft mixture of local, mid-range, and large-context residual experts for density-routed multi-receptive-field processing. An EBC-style head reconstructs block density with DMCount supervision plus an auxiliary routing-balance term, all without updating the backbone.

Result: On NWPU-Crowd validation, the DINOv3 ViT-L/16 configuration achieves 31.7 MAE and 72.2 RMSE; the matched ViT-B/16 full model obtains 32.2 MAE and 75.9 RMSE. Cross-dataset results remain mixed, with component baselines reporting independently selected minima from a single seed.

Conclusion: The results support the feasibility of spatially adaptive fusion and routing for crowd counting, but broader paired and multi-seed evaluations are necessary to establish causal attribution and confirm robustness across datasets.

Abstract: Crowd counting must recover reliable local density under severe variations in perspective, head scale, occlusion, and background clutter. Although modern counting objectives provide strong spatial supervision, many multi-level decoders still use spatially invariant feature fusion and apply one receptive-field pattern to every location. We propose DCA-MoE, a framework that makes both decisions content dependent while retaining a frozen DINOv3 encoder. Spatially Adaptive Layer Fusion (SALF) predicts position-wise weights over four aligned backbone features, and Density-Routed Multi-Receptive-Field Experts (DR-MoE) assigns each location a soft mixture of local, mid-range, and large-context residual experts. An EBC-style head reconstructs block density, while DMCount supervision and an auxiliary routing-balance term train the decoder without updating the backbone. On the NWPU-Crowd validation split, the strongest paired configuration, based on DINOv3 ViT-L/16, obtains 31.7 MAE and 72.2 RMSE; the matched ViT-B/16 full model obtains a paired 32.2/75.9. Cross-dataset results remain mixed, and several component baselines currently report independently selected minima from a single seed. The evidence therefore supports the feasibility of spatially adaptive fusion and routing, while broader paired and multi-seed evaluation remains necessary for causal attribution.

</details>


### [64] [Self-Supervised Topologically Invariant Manifold Learning for Railway Image Quality Assessment](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.15217)
*Tingqiong Cui,Yibu Yang,Yang Li,Jiahao Fu,Xiaoliu Luo,Xu Wang,Mengzhu Wang,Siyuan Liu,Guanghui Huang*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: A fully self-supervised BIQA framework using topologically invariant manifold learning under boundary constraints, generating pseudo-ground-truth quality labels without human annotations via progressive background dilution, and validated across synthetic, wild, and industrial datasets.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing BIQA methods depend on synthetic distortions and subjective annotations, which limits their generalization to real-world domains where such labeled data is unavailable or misaligned with actual viewing conditions.

Method: The framework (1) generates progressive background dilution scales via repeated random cropping to exploit monotonic degradation of target information density; (2) uses linearized spatial moment projection to remove geometric distortions from cropping; (3) applies a monotonicity divergence filter to prune background-sensitive evaluators, forming an elite pool; (4) employs a robust M-estimator with a principal component stabilizer to fuse metrics into pseudo-ground-truth quality labels contracting variance toward the Cramér-Rao lower bound.

Result: The elite evaluator pool distilled from 11 baseline metrics achieves superior zero-shot transferability on CSIQ, LIVEC, and LIVE-2 benchmarks. On the industrial CQU Railway Rolling Stock Surveillance Dataset (2,797 images), it attains manifold cosine similarity >0.999 and 100.0% survival rate under extreme stresses.

Conclusion: The proposed self-supervised framework provides a label-free alternative to conventional BIQA, demonstrating strong cross-paradigm decoupling, topological resilience, and robust generalization across both standard and industrial real-world scenarios.

Abstract: Existing blind image quality assessment (BIQA) methods typically rely on synthetic distortions and subjective annotations, limiting generalization in real-world domains. To address this, we propose a fully self-supervised BIQA framework based on topologically invariant manifold learning under boundary constraints, which constructs a stable quality reference without manual labels. The framework generates progressive background dilution scales via repeated random cropping around each target; exploiting the monotonic degradation of target information density across these scales, it establishes a self-constrained quality manifold. A linearized spatial moment projection eliminates geometric distortions from random cropping; then a monotonicity divergence filter prunes background-sensitive evaluators, isolating an elite pool \(\mathcal{M}_{\text{elite}}\). A robust M-estimator with a principal component stabilizer fuses the metrics into an asymptotically efficient pseudo-ground truth \(q_{\text{PGT}}\), contracting variance toward the Cramér-Rao lower bound. Extensive evaluations demonstrate that the elite evaluator pool, distilled from 11 baseline metrics, secures superior zero-shot transferability across standard synthetic and wild benchmarks (CSIQ, LIVEC, LIVE-2). Concurrently, deployments on the CQU Railway Rolling Stock Surveillance Dataset (2,797 images) yield a manifold cosine similarity \(>0.999\) and a 100.0\% survival rate under industrial extreme stresses, robustly validating its cross-paradigm decoupling and topological resilience.

</details>


### [65] [PersonaDrive: Controllable Trajectory Prediction with Multi-Dimensional Driving Personas](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.15230)
*Chan Lee,Kimin Yun,Yuseok Bae,Seong Tae Kim,Jung Uk Kim*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: This paper introduces the Persona-Conditioned Trajectory (PCT) dataset and PersonaDrive framework for controllable, persona-specific trajectory prediction and autonomous driving. PCT decomposes driving personas across two orthogonal axes (Temporal Urgency and Ride Comfort) into a 3x3 grid of nine personas with language descriptions and trajectories. PersonaDrive uses Persona-Conditioned Anchor Transform (PCAT) and Persona-Conditioned Multi-Modal Fusion (PCMF), trained with Hierarchical Guide Loss and Axis-Decomposed Diversity Loss, achieving consistent improvements over baselines.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing trajectory prediction and end-to-end driving methods lack meaningful controllability. Current benchmarks either lack persona-conditioned annotations or support only a single urgency spectrum, which cannot distinguish personas sharing the same urgency level but requiring different driving dynamics (e.g., comfort differences).

Method: The paper proposes (1) the PCT dataset, decomposing personas along Temporal Urgency and Ride Comfort axes into a 3x3 grid of nine personas with paired natural-language descriptions and trajectories, and (2) PersonaDrive, a framework learning personas from language. PersonaDrive includes Persona-Conditioned Anchor Transform (PCAT) for hierarchical anchor reshaping along both axes, and Persona-Conditioned Multi-Modal Fusion (PCMF) for BEV-level persona fusion. Training uses Hierarchical Guide Loss (enforcing axis-aligned physical orderings) and Axis-Decomposed Diversity Loss (preventing diagonal mode collapse).

Result: Experimental results show PersonaDrive consistently outperforms compared baselines across multi-dimensional scenarios, demonstrating the effectiveness of two-axis persona decomposition and the proposed framework in generating persona-specific trajectories.

Conclusion: The two-axis persona decomposition enables finer-grained controllability beyond single urgency spectrum, and PersonaDrive effectively learns and generates persona-specific trajectories from language, with code and dataset publicly released.

Abstract: Although recent trajectory prediction and end-to-end autonomous driving methods improve robustness in urban environments, they still lack meaningful controllability. Existing benchmarks either provide no persona-conditioned annotations or support only a single urgency spectrum (i.e., emergency, normal, relaxed), which cannot distinguish personas that share the same urgency level but require different driving dynamics. To address this, we propose (i) the Persona-Conditioned Trajectory (PCT) dataset, which decomposes driving personas along two axes, Temporal Urgency and Ride Comfort, and combines three levels of each to form a grid of nine personas, each paired with natural-language descriptions and trajectories, and (ii) PersonaDrive, a framework that can learn driving personas from language and can generate persona-specific trajectories. PersonaDrive incorporates Persona-Conditioned Anchor Transform (PCAT), which hierarchically reshapes anchors along both axes, and Persona-Conditioned Multi-Modal Fusion (PCMF) for BEV-level persona fusion. Training is supervised by a Hierarchical Guide Loss enforcing axis-aligned physical orderings and an Axis-Decomposed Diversity Loss preventing diagonal mode collapse. Experimental results show that PersonaDrive consistently improves over the compared baselines across multi-dimensional scenarios. The code and PCT dataset are available at https://github.com/VisualAIKHU/PersonaDrive

</details>


### [66] [UC-VLM: Consistency-Driven Learning for AI-Generated Image Detection with Vision-Language Large Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.15238)
*Lei Tan,Shuwei Li,Mohan Kankanhalli,Robby T. Tan*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: UC-VLM is a unified multi-stage framework for AI-generated image detection that relies solely on binary supervision, reusing authenticity labels for both visual discrimination and label-conditioned text generation while automatically optimizing instructions to avoid hand-crafted prompts and human-annotated rationales.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing VLLM-based AIGI detectors primarily fine-tune the language side while neglecting low-level visual forensic cues, and they depend on manually crafted prompts or human-annotated rationales, which limits scalability.

Method: UC-VLM employs a multi-stage training framework that (1) automatically identifies effective instruction variants, (2) uses a visual discrimination objective to strengthen sensitivity to non-semantic forensic cues, and (3) uses a label-conditioned generation objective where the binary label supervises textual outputs—turning weak binary supervision into a shared signal for both visual and language pathways.

Result: UC-VLM achieves 96.1% average accuracy on GenImage (4.6% improvement over prior SOTA) and 69.6%/77.9% accuracy on Chameleon under ProGAN/SDV1.4 training (11.2%/15.3% improvements over baselines).

Conclusion: A unified multi-stage binary-supervised framework that reuses the same authenticity labels for visual adaptation and label-conditioned text generation, with automatically optimized instructions, can achieve strong AIGI detection without requiring human-written rationales or hand-crafted prompts.

Abstract: Vision-Language Large Models (VLLMs) are promising for AI-generated image (AIGI) detection because they can produce both a prediction and a natural-language output. However, most existing VLLM-based detectors primarily fine-tune the language side while giving limited attention to low-level visual forensic cues. They also often depend on manually crafted prompts or human-annotated rationales, which limits scalability.We present UC-VLM, a unified multi-stage framework for AIGI detection that relies solely on binary supervision. UC-VLM first identifies effective instruction variants automatically. It then reuses the same binary label within a multi-stage training framework: (i) a visual discrimination objective that strengthens sensitivity to non-semantic forensic cues, and (ii) a label-conditioned generation objective that uses the binary label to supervise textual outputs. This design turns weak binary supervision into a shared supervision signal for both the visual pathway and the language output. Our key novelty is a unified multi-stage binary-supervised framework that consistently reuses the same authenticity labels for visual adaptation and label-conditioned text generation, while leveraging automatically optimized instructions to reduce prompt sensitivity without requiring human-written rationales or hand-crafted prompts.Experiments show that UC-VLM achieves 96.1% average accuracy on GenImage, exceeding the strongest prior result by 4.6%, and obtains 69.6% / 77.9% accuracy on Chameleon under ProGAN / SDV1.4 training, surpassing the best baseline by 11.2% / 15.3%, respectively.

</details>


### [67] [CG-GLORE: A Conjugate Gradient-Based Global-Local Regularization Network for Sparse-View CT Reconstruction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.15246)
*Tran Xuan Hieu Le,Doanh C. Bui,Vu Trung Duong Le,Hoai Luan Pham,Khang Nguyen,Mai K. Nguyen,Tu Bao Ho,Yasuhiko Nakashima*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: CG-GLORE is a compact deep unrolling framework for sparse-view CT reconstruction that uses second-order-inspired optimization (CG-solved linear systems with a structured Hessian surrogate) combined with a Global-Local Regularization Network (GLORE) that fuses convolutional local features with Nyström attention-based long-range dependencies. It achieves strong quantitative performance, stable convergence, lower noise power, and improved visual fidelity on AAPM and DeepLesion datasets.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Sparse-view CT reduces radiation dose but produces severe streak artifacts due to a highly ill-posed inverse problem. Existing deep reconstruction methods rely on first-order updates or large regularization networks, which are less effective in ill-conditioned settings, motivating a second-order-inspired approach.

Method: CG-GLORE unrolls iterative stages where each stage solves a linear system via conjugate gradient (CG) using a structured Hessian surrogate: it retains the physics-induced curvature of the data-fidelity term while approximating the learned regularization term's Hessian with identity. The image prior is modeled by GLORE, which combines convolutional local feature extraction with a Long-Range Dependency Representation module using sparse patchification and Nyström attention.

Result: Experiments on AAPM and DeepLesion across multiple sparse-view and noise settings show CG-GLORE achieves strong quantitative performance, stable convergence, lower noise power, and improved visual fidelity compared with representative reconstruction methods.

Conclusion: CG-GLORE demonstrates that second-order-inspired unrolling with a compact regularization network effectively addresses ill-conditioned sparse-view CT reconstruction, balancing reconstruction quality, convergence stability, and practical computational complexity.

Abstract: Sparse-view computed tomography (CT) reduces radiation dose by acquiring fewer projection views, but the resulting inverse problem is highly ill-posed and often produces severe streak artifacts. Existing deep reconstruction methods have achieved promising performance, yet many rely on first-order updates or large regularization networks, which can be less effective in ill-conditioned settings. We propose \textbf{CG-GLORE}, a compact deep unrolling framework inspired by second-order optimization for sparse-view CT reconstruction. Each unrolled stage uses a CG-solved linear system based on a structured Hessian surrogate: it retains the physics-induced curvature of the data-fidelity term while using an identity approximation for the learned regularization term. Thus, the method is second-order-inspired rather than an exact Newton method for the full learned objective. To model image priors, we design a Global-Local Regularization Network (GLORE), which combines convolutional local feature extraction with a Long-Range Dependency Representation module based on sparse patchification and Nyström attention. This design captures anatomical details and non-local dependencies while maintaining practical complexity. Experiments on AAPM and DeepLesion under multiple sparse-view and noise settings show that CG-GLORE achieves strong quantitative performance, stable convergence, lower noise power, and improved visual fidelity compared with representative reconstruction methods.

</details>


### [68] [Robust structure from motion for aerial-ground images via detector-free feature matching and multi-view track refinement](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.15251)
*San Jiang,Hui Wang,Xing Zhang,Zhongwen Hu,Zhijun Wang,Ruisheng Wang,Wanshou Jiang,Qingquan Li*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: This paper proposes a rotation-robust detector-free matching network combined with multi-view track refinement for incremental Structure from Motion (ISfM), addressing the challenge of robust feature matching in aerial-ground 3D reconstruction under severe viewpoint, scale, and rotation variations.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Integrated 3D reconstruction from aerial-ground images is critical for high-precision urban 3D models, but severe variations in viewpoint, scale, and rotation make robust feature matching extremely challenging with existing methods.

Method: The proposed workflow consists of four key modules: (1) rotation-aware feature extraction using an Omnidirectional State Space Block (OSS Block) that scans across eight symmetrical directions for rotation-invariant features; (2) multi-scale attention transformation using quadtree attention to build a hierarchical token pyramid with linear computational complexity; (3) bi-directional feature matching via a symmetric coarse-to-fine scheme with dual-direction Softmax confidence matrices and MLP-based sub-pixel coordinate regression; (4) multi-view track refinement using an integrated indexing structure to link disjoint sub-tracks to highest-confidence anchor points.

Result: Experimental results on real aerial-ground datasets show that the proposed method improves AUC at 5° pose error by 93.9% compared with LoFTR, and achieves the highest precision in ISfM reconstruction with improved accuracy ranging from 27.6% to 32.7%.

Conclusion: The proposed method provides a reliable solution for integrated 3D reconstruction of aerial-ground images by ensuring stable feature repeatability across the entire ISfM pipeline.

Abstract: Integrated 3D reconstruction from aerial-ground images is essential for generating high-precision urban 3D models, yet severe variations in viewpoint, scale, and rotation make robust feature matching highly challenging. To address these limitations, this study introduces a rotation-robust detector-free matching network coupled with multi-view track refinement for incremental Structure from Motion (ISfM). The proposed workflow features four key modules. First, rotation-aware feature extraction replaces traditional convolutions with an Omnidirectional State Space Block (OSS Block) that selectively scans across eight symmetrical directions to model long-range spatial dependencies and synthesize rotation-invariant feature maps. Second, multi-scale attention transformation utilizes quadtree attention to build a hierarchical token pyramid that isolates high-association token regions and discards irrelevant areas, capturing long-range context with linear computational complexity. Third, bi-directional feature matching executes a symmetric coarse-to-fine matching scheme where coarse alignment computes dual-direction Softmax confidence matrices under mutual nearest neighbor constraints, and fine alignment uses a multi-layer perceptron to regress sub-pixel coordinate offsets. Finally, multi-view track refinement employs an integrated indexing structure to evaluate localized spatial proximity and link disjoint sub-tracks to the highest-confidence anchor point, ensuring stable feature repeatability across the ISfM pipeline. By using real aerial-ground datasets, experimental results demonstrate that the proposed method improves AUC at 5° pose error by 93.9% compared with LoFTR and achieves the highest precision in ISfM reconstruction, with the improved accuracy ranging from 27.6% to 32.7%. The proposed method provides a reliable solution for integrated 3D reconstruction of aerial-ground images.

</details>


### [69] [UAV Video Deblurring via Motion-Aware Diffusion: A Path to Robust Target Detection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.15259)
*Zhiqiang Hu,Shouren Huang,Masatoshi Ishikawa*

Main category: cs.CV
