TL;DR: TR-GS is a Gaussian-splatting framework for sparse-view CT volumetric rendering that replaces standard Gaussian primitives with projectable Student's t-distribution primitives, introduces a ray-confidence model to regulate their degrees of freedom, and uses confidence-guided 3D wavelet regularization to balance detail preservation and noise suppression, improving over representative baselines on synthetic and real-world datasets.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Sparse-view CT reduces projection requirements and radiation exposure but suffers from structural artifacts and reconstruction uncertainty. Existing 3DGS-based CT methods using standard Gaussian primitives are sensitive to unreliable observations under sparse-view acquisition.

Method: TR-GS replaces standard Gaussian primitives with projectable Student's t-distribution primitives, introduces a ray-confidence model to regulate their degrees of freedom based on local ray observability, and applies confidence-guided 3D wavelet regularization to balance high-frequency detail preservation with noise suppression.

Result: Experiments on synthetic and real-world datasets show TR-GS improves over representative baselines in most evaluated settings and remains competitive in the remaining cases.

Conclusion: TR-GS provides a robust framework for sparse-view CT volumetric rendering that may support downstream medical multimedia applications such as XR-based visualization and interactive clinical rendering.

Abstract: High-fidelity 3D medical visualization supports applications such as clinical assessment and surgical planning. Sparse-view computed tomography (CT) can reduce projection requirements and associated radiation exposure, but limited observations may introduce structural artifacts and reconstruction uncertainty. Although 3D Gaussian Splatting (3DGS) provides an efficient explicit representation for volumetric rendering, existing CT methods based on standard Gaussian primitives may be sensitive to unreliable observations under sparse-view acquisition. We present TR-GS, a Gaussian-splatting framework for sparse view CT volumetric rendering. TR-GS replaces standard Gaussian primitives with projectable Student's t-distribution primitives and introduces a ray-confidence model that regulates their degrees of freedom according to local ray observability. Confidence-guided 3D wavelet regularization is further used to balance high-frequency detail preservation and noise suppression. This work is licensed under a Creative Commons Attribution 4.0 International License. Experiments on synthetic and real-world datasets show that TR-GS improves over representative baselines in most evaluated settings and remains competitive in the remaining cases. The resulting volumetric representations may support downstream medical multimedia applications, including XR-based visualization and interactive clinical rendering.

</details>


### [139] [SafeGesture: Evaluating Fine-Grained Hand Gesture Understanding in Vision-Language Models through Scenario-Conditioned Safety Interpretation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.16081)
*Taegang Kim,Saleh Afroogh,Junfeng Jiao*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: SafeGesture is a benchmark evaluating whether vision-language models can infer scenario-appropriate safety actions from hand gestures. Tests across five VLMs reveal a perception-reasoning decoupling: models achieve high gesture recognition accuracy but significantly lower safety reasoning accuracy, indicating the primary bottleneck is scenario-conditioned safety reasoning rather than gesture recognition.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: While open-weight and frontier VLMs perform well on general image understanding, their ability to interpret fine-grained hand gestures in safety-critical operational contexts remains largely unexamined. This gap is important because gesture understanding in operational settings requires not just recognizing the gesture but also inferring the scenario-appropriate safety action.

Method: The authors introduce SafeGesture, a benchmark pairing six HaGRID gestures with eight operational scenarios to create 4,800 items. They evaluate five VLMs: Qwen2.5-VL-7B, LLaVA-NeXT-7B, InternVL2-8B, Phi-3.5-Vision, and GPT-4o, measuring both gesture accuracy and scenario-conditioned safety accuracy.

Result: GPT-4o achieves 98.4% gesture accuracy but only 53.3% safety accuracy (45.0-point gap), while Qwen2.5-VL reaches 84.9% gesture and 39.5% safety accuracy (45.4-point gap). Four of five models rarely or never use the uncertainty label. A scenario-majority policy with no visual input reaches 58.3%, exceeding all evaluated models except GPT-4o under macro-F1. Visual input improves safety accuracy by 11.2-30.2 points, but providing ground-truth gestures as text improves performance by only 0.4-3.2 points, with no model exceeding 56.2%.

Conclusion: The main bottleneck in safety-critical gesture interpretation is scenario-conditioned safety reasoning rather than gesture recognition itself. Even with perfect gesture recognition (via text-provided ground truth), models fail to approach acceptable safety accuracy, indicating a fundamental reasoning deficiency that needs to be addressed for deployment in safety-critical settings.

Abstract: Open-weight and frontier vision-language models (VLMs) perform well on general image understanding, but their ability to interpret fine-grained hand gestures in safety-critical operational contexts remains largely unexamined. We introduce SafeGesture, a benchmark that evaluates whether a model can infer scenario-appropriate safety actions from hand gestures. It pairs six HaGRID gestures with eight operational scenarios for 4,800 items and evaluates Qwen2.5-VL-7B, LLaVA-NeXT-7B, InternVL2-8B, Phi-3.5-Vision, and GPT-4o. Results reveal a perception-reasoning decoupling: GPT-4o achieves 98.4% gesture accuracy but 53.3% safety accuracy, while Qwen2.5-VL reaches 84.9% and 39.5%, yielding gaps of 45.0 and 45.4 percentage points. Four of five models rarely or never use the uncertainty label, and failure directions differ substantially across models. Accuracy also obscures label bias: a scenario-majority policy with no visual input reaches 58.3%, above every evaluated model, while only GPT-4o exceeds this prior under macro-F1. Visual input improves safety accuracy by 11.2 to 30.2 percentage points, but providing the ground-truth gesture as text improves performance by only 0.4 to 3.2 points, and no model exceeds 56.2%. These results indicate that the main bottleneck is scenario-conditioned safety reasoning rather than gesture recognition.

</details>


### [140] [Representation Is Not Enough: Body-Localized Thermal Evidence for Contactless Stress and Craving Sensing in Opioid Use Disorder](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.16087)
*Sachin Deb,Harshit Sharma,Asif Salekin*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: FABLE-Therm is a weakly supervised architecture for contactless stress sensing from thermal video that preserves localized evidence across body regions, time, and encoder-specific representations until the final decision. It achieves 0.938 AUROC on held-out OUD participants and demonstrates the first evidence that craving can be recovered from contactless thermal video, while also enabling principled analysis of model deployment failures across subpopulations.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Removing wearables from physiological monitoring removes supervision signals that indicate where and when stress responses occur. This turns contactless stress sensing into a weakly supervised evidence-localization problem where clip-level labels must be traced to specific body regions and moments. Additionally, in OUD, stress is a major relapse trigger and sustained wearable use is difficult during early recovery, motivating the need for contactless approaches.

Method: FABLE-Therm is a weakly supervised architecture that preserves localized evidence across body regions, time, and encoder-specific representations until the final decision. It fuses frozen foundation-model encoders at the embedding level, with a theoretical justification for why localized fusion outperforms feature concatenation and prediction averaging.

Result: FABLE-Therm achieves 0.938 AUROC on held-out participants using fixed thermal video. Its learned representation transfers to self-reported craving, providing the first evidence that craving can be recovered from contactless thermal video. The localized evidence enables participant-level analysis of deployment failure, revealing that improving representation alone is insufficient for equitable deployment—additional data from the underserved group recovers only about half of the cohort gap.

Conclusion: Preserving localized evidence supports both accurate sensing and principled analysis of who a model fails and why. The modality-agnostic decomposition of cohort gaps applies to models with identifiable subpopulations, and the first cohort-structured contactless thermal OUD benchmark is introduced.

Abstract: Removing wearables from physiological monitoring also removes their supervision: the signal indicating where and when a stress response occurred. Contactless stress sensing therefore becomes a weakly supervised evidence-localization problem, where a clip-level label must be traced to the body regions and moments that produced it. We address this with FABLE-Therm, a weakly supervised architecture that preserves localized evidence across body regions, time, and encoder-specific representations until the final decision. FABLE-Therm fuses frozen foundation-model encoders at the embedding level, with theory explaining why localized fusion can outperform feature concatenation and prediction averaging. We study this problem in opioid use disorder (OUD), where stress is a major relapse trigger and sustained wearable use can be difficult during early recovery. Using fixed thermal video, FABLE-Therm achieves 0.938 AUROC on held-out participants, and its learned representation transfers to self-reported craving, providing, to our knowledge, the first evidence that craving can be recovered from contactless thermal video. Localized evidence also enables participant-level analysis of deployment failure. We find that improving representation alone is insufficient for equitable deployment: additional data from the underserved group would recover only about half of the cohort gap, while the remainder reflects person-to-person heterogeneity. This modality-agnostic decomposition applies to models with identifiable subpopulations. Together with the first cohort-structured contactless thermal OUD benchmark, our results show that preserving localized evidence supports both accurate sensing and principled analysis of who a model fails and why.

</details>


### [141] [TISC: A Text-Driven Image Semantic Communication System for Faithful Reconstruction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.16100)
*Feifan Zhang,Yuyang Du,Xiaoyan Liu,Soung Chang Liew*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: TISC is a text-driven image semantic communication framework that addresses semantic faithfulness bottlenecks via tree-structured attribute semantic extraction (TSASE) for detailed I2T description and initial noise optimization (INO) for reliable T2I reconstruction.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing generative image semantic communication methods suffer from two key bottlenecks: (i) holistic I2T descriptions lose fine-grained object attributes and spatial-position details, and (ii) T2I reconstruction faithfulness varies with different initial noise settings even for identical text descriptions. These jointly limit the semantic faithfulness of reconstructed images.

Method: TISC introduces two components: (1) TSASE (Tree-Structured Attribute Semantic Extraction) decomposes semantic extraction into global scene, background, and object-level attribute descriptions covering spatial, shape/pose, color, material and other physical attributes per object; (2) INO (Initial Noise Optimization) selects an initial noise seed at the transmitter based on a comprehensive similarity score jointly considering visual and semantic consistency.

Result: Experiments on multiple datasets show TSASE improves object-position recovery and semantic description faithfulness, and the INO parameter study supports the chosen configuration for noise selection.

Conclusion: The proposed TISC framework effectively enhances semantic faithfulness in text-driven image semantic communication by addressing both I2T semantic extraction loss and T2I reconstruction variability.

Abstract: Generative image semantic communication converts an image into a text description and then performs text-to-image reconstruction at the receiver via diffusion-based generative models. This paradigm has attracted broad attention due to its extremely low bandwidth cost. However, existing methods still face two critical bottlenecks across image-to-text (I2T) semantic extraction at the transmitter and text-to-image (T2I) semantic reconstruction at the receiver: (i) semantic loss and distortion in I2T, where holistic image descriptions may omit fine-grained object attributes and spatial-position information, causing the generated text to deviate from the original image semantics; and (ii) insufficient semantic faithfulness in T2I, where even with the same semantically faithful text description, different initial noise settings may lead diffusion-based reconstruction to produce images with different levels of semantic consistency with the original image. These issues jointly limit the semantic faithfulness of image reconstruction. To address them, we propose TISC, a text-driven image semantic communication framework tailored for faithful reconstruction. TISC incorporates two key designs: (1) Tree-Structured Attribute Semantic Extraction (TSASE), which decomposes semantic extraction into global scene, background, and object-level attribute descriptions, covering spatial position, shape/pose, color, material, and other physical attributes for each detected object; and (2) an Initial Noise Optimization (INO) mechanism, which selects an initial noise seed at the transmitter according to a comprehensive similarity score that jointly considers visual and semantic consistency. Experiments on multiple datasets show that TSASE improves object-position recovery and semantic description faithfulness, while the INO parameter study supports the adopted configuration for noise selection.

</details>


### [142] [Beyond Similarity Matching: Structured Reasoning for Open-Vocabulary Referring Segmentation in 3DGS](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.16103)
*Yizhao Wang,Xinfa Wang,Jingbo Wang,Jingbo Wang,Guantao Zhang,Yafeng Han,Guohong Gao,Yuhe Xia*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: QAGaussian is a query-adaptive neural reasoning framework for open-vocabulary referring segmentation in 3D Gaussian Splatting. It learns query-conditioned multi-scale Gaussian slots, builds relation-aware slot graphs with language-conditioned edge weighting, and uses a granularity-adaptive router with relation-constrained refinement. Pretrained only on Mosaic3D-5.6M and evaluated without target-dataset fine-tuning, it achieves 47.2 Avg. mIoU and 63.2 Avg. F1, outperforming the strongest 3DGS referring baseline by 2.7 mIoU and 2.9 F1 points, with notable improvements in part-level and relational reasoning.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing 3DGS-based referring segmentation methods rely on global text-region similarity, which is insufficient for queries involving attributes, reference objects, spatial relations, and fine-grained parts. This leads to target-reference confusion, granularity mismatch, part-whole leakage, and relation violations.

Method: QAGaussian learns query-conditioned multi-scale Gaussian slots as differentiable candidates shaped by input expressions, builds a relation-aware slot graph with language-conditioned edge weighting to propagate various evidence types, and uses a granularity-adaptive router that softly combines region/object/part/attribute/relation-aware mask branches, followed by relation-constrained refinement for consistency.

Result: QAGaussian achieves 47.2 Avg. mIoU and 63.2 Avg. F1, surpassing the strongest 3DGS baseline by 2.7 mIoU and 2.9 F1 points. It improves Part-mIoU from 38.6 to 43.4, Rel-mIoU from 44.4 to 50.8, and reduces target-reference confusion from 10.8 to 7.4, all without target-dataset fine-tuning.

Conclusion: Query-conditioned slot learning, relation-aware graph reasoning, and adaptive routing provide an effective neural modeling strategy for open-vocabulary referring segmentation in 3DGS, demonstrating strong generalization without target-dataset fine-tuning.

Abstract: Open-vocabulary referring segmentation in 3D Gaussian Splatting (3DGS) requires a neural model to select Gaussian primitives according to free-form language expressions. Existing 3DGS-based methods usually rely on global text-region similarity, which is weak for queries involving attributes, reference objects, spatial relations, and fine-grained parts. This often causes target-reference confusion, granularity mismatch, part-whole leakage, and relation violations. We propose QAGaussian, a query-adaptive neural reasoning framework for language-guided Gaussian primitive selection. QAGaussian first learns query-conditioned multi-scale Gaussian slots as differentiable candidates whose receptive fields are shaped by the input expression. It then builds a relation-aware slot graph with language-conditioned edge weighting to propagate target-reference, attribute, part-whole, and contextual evidence. A granularity-adaptive router softly combines region-level, object-level, part-level, attribute-aware, and relation-aware mask branches, followed by relation-constrained refinement for spatial, part-whole, attribute, and geometric consistency. QAGaussian is pretrained only on Mosaic3D-5.6M for Gaussian-text alignment and evaluated on independent benchmarks without target-dataset fine-tuning. It achieves 47.2 Avg. mIoU and 63.2 Avg. F1, outperforming the strongest 3DGS referring baseline by 2.7 mIoU points and 2.9 F1 points. It also improves Part-mIoU from 38.6 to 43.4, Rel-mIoU from 44.4 to 50.8, and reduces target-reference confusion from 10.8 to 7.4. These results demonstrate that query-conditioned slot learning, relation-aware graph reasoning, and adaptive routing provide an effective neural modeling strategy for open-vocabulary referring segmentation in 3DGS. The code is available at https://github.com/zqeslwyz/QAGaussian.

</details>


### [143] [Nexus: Structured Synergy for Efficient Text-to-Image Generation using Rectified Flow Model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.16104)
*Yizhao Wang*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: Nexus is an efficient text-to-image diffusion model integrating sparse MoE layers, linear-complexity DeltaNet attention, and low-bit quantization to reduce computation and memory, achieving quality comparable to SDXL/SD3 with significantly higher inference efficiency.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Diffusion and flow matching models for text-to-image generation suffer from high computation cost, quadratic complexity in attention, and large memory footprint, which hinder high-resolution synthesis and edge deployment.

Method: Nexus integrates three key components: (1) MoE feed-forward layers for sparse computation, (2) gated DeltaNet attention for linear complexity, and (3) per-expert low-bit training for quantization, jointly optimized to reduce both computation and memory without sacrificing generation quality.

Result: Nexus achieves generation quality comparable to mainstream models such as SDXL and SD3, while delivering markedly higher inference efficiency. Experiments on COCO and LAION datasets validate its effectiveness.

Conclusion: The joint optimization of sparse architecture, linear attention, and low-bit quantization enables efficient high-quality text-to-image generation, making it suitable for resource-constrained and edge deployment scenarios.

Abstract: Diffusion and flow matching models have made significant progress in text-to-image generation, yet high computation, quadratic complexity, and large memory footprint hinder high-resolution synthesis and edge deployment. We propose Nexus, which integrates sparse architecture, linear complexity, and low-bit quantization. It combines MoE feed-forward layers, gated DeltaNet attention, and per-expert low-bit training to reduce computation and memory. Their joint optimization allows Nexus to achieve generation quality comparable to mainstream models such as SDXL and SD3 while delivering markedly higher inference efficiency. Experiments on COCO and LAION validate its effectiveness.

</details>


### [144] [SUGFW+: An Uncertainty-guided Feature Weighting Framework for Cold Start Active Adaptation of SAM in Medical Image Segmentation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.16110)
*Xiaochuan Ma,Ning Zhu,Jia Fu,Lanfeng Zhong,Hanyu Jiang,Bin Song,Kang Li,Guotai Wang*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: SUGFW+ is a framework for Cold Start Active Learning (CSAL) in medical image segmentation that leverages Segment Anything Model (SAM) embeddings for sample selection and adaptation. It uses Patch-level Feature and Uncertainty Calculation, a Patch-based Global Distinct Representation module to aggregate patch embeddings into uncertainty-aware image-level features, and a Greedy Selection with Cluster and Uncertainty strategy for joint diversity-uncertainty sample selection. Unlike prior methods, it tightly integrates sample selection with model training via Uncertainty-Prompted Fine-Tuning. Experiments on four public datasets show state-of-the-art performance.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing CSAL methods rely on dataset-specific Self-Supervised Learning (SSL) for feature extraction, which is inefficient. Foundation models like SAM offer strong generalizable embeddings, but their systematic exploitation for cold-start sample selection during low-budget adaptation remains underexplored.

Method: The framework leverages SAM for Patch-level Feature and Uncertainty Calculation (PFUC), a Patch-based Global Distinct Representation (PGDR) module to aggregate patch-level features into discriminative uncertainty-aware image-level features, a Greedy Selection with Cluster and Uncertainty (GSCU) strategy for sample selection, and Uncertainty-Prompted Fine-Tuning (UPFT) to integrate selection with model training.

Result: Extensive experiments on four public medical imaging datasets demonstrate SUGFW+ achieves state-of-the-art performance compared to existing CSAL methods.

Conclusion: SAM's inherent embeddings can be effectively exploited for cold-start active learning in medical image segmentation, and tightly coupling sample selection with adaptation (fine-tuning) improves performance under low annotation budgets.

Abstract: Cold Start Active Learning (CSAL) is important in improving the performance of a medical image segmentation model with low annotation budget by querying a small subset for annotation from an unlabeled training set. Existing CSAL methods typically rely on inefficient dataset-specific Self-Supervised Learning (SSL) to map the unlabeled images into a feature space for sample selection. Recently, the advent of foundation models such as the Segment Anything Model (SAM) offer a promising alternative as the pre-trained model can provide strong generalizable feature embeddings, and allow high performance in downstream tasks after fine-tuning (adaptation). However, how to systematically exploit SAM's inherent embeddings for cold-start sample selection during adaptation with low annotation budget remains underexplored. To address this, we propose an extended SAM-based Uncertainty-guided Feature Weighting (SUGFW+) framework for CSAL and adaptation of SAM. Specifically, it leverages the SAM for Patch-level Feature and Uncertainty Calculation (PFUC), and introduces a Patch-based Global Distinct Representation (PGDR) module that aggregates patch-level embeddings into highly discriminative, uncertainty-aware image-level features. These features are then utilized by a Greedy Selection with Cluster and Uncertainty (GSCU) strategy to combine diversity and uncertainty during sample selection. Unlike prior CSAL methods that decouple sample selection from model training, SUGFW+ tightly integrates these two stages via an Uncertainty-Prompted Fine-Tuning (UPFT) process of SAM in model training. Extensive experiments on four public datasets demonstrate that SUGFW+ achieves state-of-the-art performance against existing CSAL methods. Code is available at https://github.com/HiLab-git/SUGFW-plus.

</details>


### [145] [TokenSTFormer: A Tokenized Spatial-temporal Attention Model for Holistic Motion Analysis in Adolescent Idiopathic Scoliosis Screening](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.16122)
*Dong Chen,Kenneth M. C. Cheung*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: The paper presents ScoliGait, a dataset of 1,516 gait video clips paired with X-ray records, and TokenSTFormer, a novel model that tokenizes spatial and temporal semantics for Adolescent Idiopathic Scoliosis (AIS) screening. The model achieves state-of-the-art accuracy of 0.79, surpassing vanilla Vision Transformer, demonstrating the potential of gait-based motion analysis for scalable, cost-effective scoliosis screening.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Traditional AIS screening methods suffer from subjective interpretation, dependence on professional expertise, and limited scalability, necessitating a more objective and scalable screening approach.

Method: The authors introduce the ScoliGait dataset containing 1,516 gait video clips paired with X-ray records, and propose TokenSTFormer, a model that tokenizes spatial and temporal semantics to improve feature representation and convergence for AIS classification from gait videos.

Result: The proposed model achieves state-of-the-art performance with 0.79 accuracy, outperforming the vanilla Vision Transformer encoder across key evaluation metrics.

Conclusion: Holistic motion features from gait videos combined with attention-based models offer a scalable, cost-effective alternative for AIS screening, with potential for future clinical applications in scoliosis detection.

Abstract: Adolescent Idiopathic Scoliosis (AIS) is a prevalent spinal deformity in adolescents that, if left untreated, can result in severe health outcomes. Traditional screening methods are limited by subjective interpretation, reliance on professional expertise and low scalability. To address these challenges, we present ScoliGait dataset, which comprises 1,516 gait video clips paired with corresponding X-ray records. We also introduce TokenSTFormer, a novel model that tokenizes spatial and temporal semantics to enhance feature representation and convergence. Our model achieves state-of-the-art performance, surpassing vanilla Vision Transformer encoder across key metrics, including accuracy of 0.79. This study highlights the potential of leveraging holistic motion features derived from gait video and attention-based models for scalable, cost-effective AIS screening, paving the way for future clinical applications in scoliosis detection.

</details>


### [146] [Graph Neural Assisted Actor-Critic for Latency-Efficient Edge Vision System](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.16142)
*Alam Noor,Luis Almeida,Kai Li,Jiyan Wu,Miguel Gutiérrez Gaitán,Eduardo Tovar*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: The paper proposes a GCN-assisted A2C deep reinforcement learning system to optimize UAV video transmission latency by transmitting only pixel-correlated sub-groups of video frames instead of full frames, reducing both transmission latency and false detection rates.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: UAV on-board vision systems streaming video to ground servers suffer from high transmission latency, which negatively impacts operator assistance effectiveness. Existing video transmission techniques incur significant latency costs that need to be addressed.

Method: The proposed framework combines a Graph Convolutional Neural Network (GCN) with A2C deep reinforcement learning. GCN explores hidden representations of feature-correlated pixel groups, while A2C selects an optimal sub-group of pixels to transmit. Lagrangian dual form with gradient descent is used to prevent convergence issues and constraint violation during latency optimization.

Result: Experimental results demonstrate that the GCN-assisted A2C model reduces video frame transmission latency and false detection rates compared to other DRL and state-of-the-art models in UAV vision systems.

Conclusion: The proposed GCN-assisted A2C framework effectively optimizes latency in UAV video transmission by transmitting only relevant pixel sub-groups, improving both transmission efficiency and detection accuracy over existing approaches.

Abstract: UAV on-board vision systems are widely used for different activities, including monitoring in no-fly zones. In this case, the vision-equipped UAV streams a video to a ground server where an operator assists its activities. The latency of video transmission has a profound impact on the effectiveness of the operator assistance. However, most techniques available for video transmission still incur significant latency costs. In this paper, we propose a graph convolutional neural network-assisted (GCN-Assisted A2C) deep reinforcement learning (DRL) system model to find the optimal pixel-correlated area of a suspicious object. We combine the Lagrangian dual form with gradient descent to prevent lack of convergence and over- and under-penalization constraint violation during latency optimization. The proposed system model sends a sub-group pixel-correlated area of the frame from the UAV to the server rather than the transmission of the whole video frame. The proposed framework utilizes the GCN model to explore hidden representations of feature-correlated groups of pixels. Moreover, the GCN supervises the A2C model, which selects a subgroup to enhance transmission latency, thus supervising the training of UAV actions in A2C. Experimental results show that GCN-assisted A2C reduces video frame transmission latency together with false detection rate in UAV vision systems over other DRL and state-of-the-art models.

</details>


### [147] [The Right Prior for the Right Deformation: Rethinking Continuous Deformable Image Registration](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.16146)
*Hengjie Liu,Chushu Shen,Dan Ruan,Ke Sheng*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: A validation study comparing continuous deformable image registration methods (B-Spline vs. INR-based) across brain MR and lung CT tasks, finding that matching the implicit deformation prior to the target motion pattern is critical for accuracy, with a multiresolution B-Spline approach (MR-D-BSCP) achieving best overall performance.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Deformable image registration models implicitly encode deformation priors through their parameterization, but systematic comparisons of how these implicit priors (e.g., B-Spline locality/smoothness vs. neural network-based priors) affect performance across different registration tasks are lacking.

Method: Compares four continuous registration parameterizations: INR-Dense (IDIR) using SIREN-based INR for dense displacement fields; INR-BSCP (SINR) predicting B-Spline control points with an INR; D-BSCP directly optimizing single-scale B-Spline control points; and MR-D-BSCP adding a multiresolution coarse-to-fine scheme. Evaluated on inter-subject brain MR (OASIS) and intra-subject lung CT (DIR-LAB 4DCT).

Result: On OASIS (moderate but locally complex deformations), D-BSCP matches or slightly outperforms INR-BSCP. On DIR-LAB 4DCT (larger, coherent respiratory motion), single-scale B-Spline methods are less suitable while INR-Dense and MR-D-BSCP are more effective. MR-D-BSCP achieves best performance across both tasks.

Conclusion: Registration accuracy depends strongly on matching the induced deformation prior to the target motion pattern, supporting prior-deformation matching as a practical design principle for medical image registration.

Abstract: Deformable image registration models implicitly encode deformation priors through their parametrization and optimization. In this work, we conduct a validation study on continuous registration methods to examine how these implicit priors affect performance across different registration tasks. Classic B-Spline transformations impose locality, smoothness, and scale through their control-point structure, whereas recent INR-based methods impose different priors through neural parameterization and optimization. We compare INR-Dense (IDIR), which directly models a dense displacement field using a SIREN-based INR; INR-BSCP (SINR), which predicts B-Spline control points with an INR; D-BSCP, which directly optimizes single-scale B-Spline control points; and MR-D-BSCP, which adds a multiresolution coarse-to-fine scheme. Experiments on inter-subject brain MR registration (OASIS) and intra-subject exhale-to-inhale lung CT registration (DIR-LAB 4DCT) reveal different behavior across deformation regimes. On OASIS, where deformations are moderate but locally complex, D-BSCP matches or slightly outperforms INR-BSCP, suggesting that the B-Spline parameterization accounts for much of INR-BSCP's effectiveness. On DIR-LAB 4DCT, where respiratory motion is larger and more coherent, single-scale B-Spline methods (D-BSCP and INR-BSCP) are less suitable, while INR-Dense and MR-D-BSCP are more effective. Across both tasks, MR-D-BSCP achieves the best performance among the tested continuous parameterizations. These findings highlight that registration accuracy depends strongly on matching the induced deformation prior to the target motion pattern, and support prior-deformation matching as a practical design principle for medical image registration. Our code will be available at https://github.com/HengjieLiu/RightPriorDIR.

</details>


### [148] [KeyID: Decoupled Drafting and Keyframe Editing for Identity-Preserving Video Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.16154)
*Jianjie Luo,Yiming Zhong,Haoming Shen,Yupeng Xiao,Zhenguo Yang*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: KeyID is a training-free identity-preserving video generation framework that decouples video dynamics synthesis from identity injection via a two-component pipeline: reference-aware video generation producing an identity-agnostic draft, followed by identity-preserved keyframe editing with sparse keyframe correction and motion interpolation. It achieves runner-up in ACM Multimedia 2026 IPVG Grand Challenge Track 2.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing IPVG methods suffer from high tuning costs or limited input-level enhancements, and struggle to maintain rigid identity consistency during complex, long-sequence actions. There is a capacity conflict between prompt adherence and identity fidelity under dense frame-level supervision.

Method: KeyID is a training-free IPVG framework with two components: (1) Reference-Aware Video Generation that produces an identity-agnostic video draft aligned with multiple references, and (2) Identity-Preserved Keyframe Editing that integrates the target identity via sparse keyframe correction followed by motion interpolation. The modular design enables extension to multi-subject references and sequential actions without training.

Result: KeyID outperforms prior works in automatic and human evaluations on the official challenge benchmark, securing runner-up position in Track 2 (Sequential Action) of the ACM Multimedia 2026 IPVG Grand Challenge.

Conclusion: Shifting from dense frame-level supervision to sparse keyframe-level refinement effectively resolves the capacity conflict between prompt adherence and identity fidelity, demonstrating that decoupling dynamics generation from identity injection is a viable training-free approach for IPVG.

Abstract: Identity-preserving video generation (IPVG) requires synthesizing videos that are faithful to both reference subjects and text prompts. Existing methods are often hindered by high tuning costs or limited input-level enhancements, struggling to maintain rigid identity consistency during complex, long-sequence actions. To address these limitations, we propose KeyID, a training-free IPVG framework that decouples the synthesis of video dynamics from the injection of identity. Specifically, KeyID comprises two components: (1) Reference-Aware Video Generation, which produces an identity-agnostic video draft aligned with multiple references, and (2) Identity-Preserved Keyframe Editing, which integrates the target identity via sparse keyframe correction and subsequent motion interpolation. By shifting from dense frame-level supervision to sparse keyframe-level refinement, KeyID effectively resolves the capacity conflict between prompt adherence and identity fidelity. Crucially, our modular design allows seamless extension to multi-subject references and complex sequential action generation without additional training. KeyID outperforms prior works and is validated by automatic and human evaluations on the official challenge benchmark, ultimately securing the runner-up position in the Track 2 (Sequential Action) of the ACM Multimedia 2026 IPVG Grand Challenge. Source code is available at https://github.com/WISLab-GDUT/KeyID.

</details>


### [149] [Beyond Clear Skies: Synthetic Seasonal and Weather Variations for Real-World Drone Detection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.16191)
*Tamara R. Lenhard,Andreas Weinmann,Tobias Koch*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: Introduces SynDroneVision-Weather (SDV-W), a large synthetic dataset (55,187 annotated high-resolution images) targeting adverse-weather and seasonal domain shifts for urban drone detection, showing improved detector reliability across representative YOLO models and real-world datasets.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Real-world drone detection requires training data spanning diverse weather and seasonal conditions, but acquiring and annotating such data at scale is resource-intensive since adverse conditions are hard to control and reproduce. Existing datasets provide limited coverage of these conditions.

Method: SDV-W systematically extends SynDroneVision using game-engine-based synthetic pipelines, rendering 55,187 annotated high-resolution images across three urban environments, three seasonal configurations, and diverse weather conditions (rain, snow, fog at multiple severity levels). It preserves SDV's scene/trajectory configuration to enable matched clean-adverse comparisons.

Result: Across representative YOLO models and real-world datasets, SDV-W improves detector reliability under adverse appearance shifts, reduces missed detections and false alarms, and is most effective as a complement to general-purpose synthetic drone-detection data.

Conclusion: Synthetic data like SDV-W provides a scalable, controllable alternative for addressing adverse-weather domain shifts in drone detection, and will be publicly released upon paper acceptance.

Abstract: Reliable drone detection under real-world deployment conditions requires training data that spans the full operational design domain, including adverse weather and seasonal appearance variation. However, acquiring and annotating such data at scale remains highly resource-intensive, as adverse-weather conditions are inherently difficult to control, reproduce, and sample systematically. Existing datasets therefore typically provide only limited coverage of such conditions. Conversely, synthetic data offers a scalable alternative: environmental variation becomes controllable, while modern game-engine-based pipelines provide realistic rendering and automatic annotations. Leveraging this potential, we introduce SynDroneVision-Weather (SDV-W), an systematic extension of SynDroneVision (SDV) targeting adverse-weather and seasonal domain shifts in urban drone detection. SDV-W comprises 55,187 annotated high-resolution images from three urban environments, rendered across three seasonal configurations and diverse weather conditions, including rain, snow, and fog at multiple severity levels. By preserving SDV's scene and trajectory configuration, SDV-W enables matched clean-adverse comparisons and quantification of condition-specific detector degradation. Across representative YOLO models and real-world datasets, we show that SDV-W improves detector reliability under adverse appearance shifts, reduces missed detections and false alarms, and is most effective as a complement to general-purpose synthetic drone-detection data. SDV-W will be publicly released upon paper acceptance.

</details>


### [150] [Picking the Right Image to Classify: Reliable-Input Selection in Teledermatology](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.16198)
*Fabian Gröger,Marco Weishaupt,Philippe Gottfrois,Simone Lionetti,Linda Wermelinger,Nipun Ranasekara,Ludovic Amruthalingam,Alexander A. Navarini,Marc Pouly*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: This paper introduces "reliable-input selection," a task for dermatology models facing distribution shifts in teledermatology. When multiple images of the same case exist, the goal is to select the image most likely to be classified correctly. An oracle selector that knows labels can improve weighted F1 by ~20 points, but all tested training-data-free selectors (embedding norm, neighborhood consensus, perturbation stability, confidence) and even a fusion with Mahalanobis distance recover only a small fraction of the oracle gap. The task remains unsolved.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Dermatology models degrade under distribution shifts in teledermatology (lighting, angle, focus, framing). Since multiple photos of the same case are often available, selecting the image the model is most likely to classify correctly is a natural way to improve accuracy. The oracle upper bound shows ~20 point F1 gains are possible, motivating the need for practical selectors.

Method: The paper defines reliable-input selection and benchmarks four training-data-free selectors: (1) embedding norm, (2) neighborhood consensus among a case's images, (3) prediction stability under perturbations, and (4) model confidence. They evaluate across six dermatology datasets and nine frozen backbones, also testing a fusion of confidence with Mahalanobis distance using a small labeled reference set.

Result: The oracle selector improves weighted F1 by ~20 percentage points on average. However, no training-data-free selector substantially narrows this oracle gap. Model confidence is the best of the four but recovers only a small part of the gap. The fusion with Mahalanobis distance (best overall) still leaves most of the gap unclosed.

Conclusion: Reliable-input selection is a clinically important but unsolved task. Despite a large oracle potential, existing data-free and reference-based selectors fail to capture most of the gain, highlighting the need for further research on practical selection strategies for dermatology models under distribution shift.

Abstract: Dermatology models face distribution shifts in teledermatology settings, where submitted images differ from the training data in lighting, angle, distance, focus, and framing. These test-time images are ordinary clinical photographs, but some fall outside the model's training conditions, leading the model to often misclassify them due to shifts in acquisition between training and deployment. When multiple images of the same case exist (several photos of one patient or lesion), a natural way to improve accuracy is therefore to select the image the model is most likely to classify correctly. We call this task reliable-input selection. An oracle that, for each case, selects a correctly classified image when one exists raises weighted F1 by about 20 percentage points on average across six dermatology datasets and nine frozen backbones. This oracle is an upper bound that sees the labels, whereas a selector must choose blindly. Capturing this gain in practice is hard. A selector that needs no pretraining data applies to any frozen model, including those whose data is not public. It must judge reliability from quantities the model exposes at inference: its embeddings, their norms, and its confidence. We benchmark four such training-data-free selectors: the embedding norm, the neighborhood consensus among a case's images, the stability of the prediction under small perturbations, and the model's own confidence. No training-data-free selector substantially narrows this oracle gap. The best of them is the model's own confidence, but it recovers only a small part of the gap on the clinical datasets. A small labeled reference set does not help either: the best selector overall, a fusion of confidence and Mahalanobis distance, still leaves most of the gap. To our knowledge, this is the first study to introduce and benchmark reliable input selection, a clinically important, unsolved task.

</details>


### [151] [PCT-Prompt: A Prompt-Guided Transformer Framework for Dense Prediction Tasks in Point Clouds](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.16225)
*Dejun Zhang,Yanzi Bai,Yiqi Wu*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: PCT-Prompt is a framework that enhances standard Transformers for point cloud dense prediction tasks by adding a prompt-guided feature branch alongside a pre-trained Transformer backbone, incorporating fine-grained feature extraction with PnP-3D layers, prompt token generation with cross-attention refinement, and a prompt drop mechanism to balance local details and global consistency.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Standard Transformers perform well on point cloud object classification but suffer in dense prediction tasks within complex scenes due to weak prior assumptions, limiting their adaptability.

Method: The framework uses a standard pre-trained Transformer branch for global feature extraction, augmented by a prompt-guided feature branch containing: (1) a fine-grained feature extraction block with geometry-sensitive abstraction layers and PnP-3D layers for multi-scale features with local-global regularization, and (2) a prompt-refined feature learning block that generates prompt tokens refined via cross-attention, plus a prompt drop mechanism that progressively removes prompts across layers.

Result: Experiments on ShapeNetPart, S3DIS, and DALES datasets show PCT-Prompt significantly improves standard Transformers' adaptability to dense prediction tasks with strong performance in real-world scenarios.

Conclusion: PCT-Prompt effectively bridges the gap between standard Transformers and dense prediction requirements in point clouds by integrating prompt-guided feature learning, achieving robust performance across diverse datasets.

Abstract: Standard Transformers have proven effective in point cloud object classification, but their performance in dense prediction tasks within complex scenes is often hindered by weak prior assumptions. To address this challenge, we propose PCT-Prompt, a novel framework that enhances standard Transformers by introducing a prompt-guided feature branch to improve performance in dense prediction tasks. The standard Transformer branch leverages pre-trained models for global feature extraction from point cloud data, serving as the backbone for processing high-level features. Meanwhile, the prompt-guided feature branch consists of two key components: a fine-grained feature extraction block that captures multi-scale geometric features using geometry-sensitive abstraction layer, along with the PnP-3D layer to integrate local context with global regularization. The second component, the prompt-refined feature learning block generates prompt tokens, which are subsequently refined through cross-attention mechanisms. Additionally, we introduce a prompt drop mechanism that progressively removes prompt information across Transformer layers, balancing local details and global consistency. Experimental results on the ShapeNetPart, S3DIS, and DALES datasets demonstrate that PCT-Prompt significantly improves the adaptability of standard Transformers to dense prediction tasks, achieving strong performance in real-world scenarios.

</details>


### [152] [GaussianDWM++: Language-Grounded 3D Gaussian Driving World Model for Unified Scene Understanding, Editing, and Multi-Modal Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.16234)
*Tianchen Deng,Xuefeng Chen,Shuang Wu,Qu Chen,Jiajun Zhu,Bo Dai,Jianfei Yang,Hesheng Wang*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: This paper proposes a foundation-feature Gaussian driving world model that unifies scene understanding, language-grounded reasoning, controllable 4D editing, and multi-modal generation in a single framework. It introduces a foundation-feature Gaussian tokenizer that distills Qwen/SigLIP visual-language features into 3D Gaussian primitives, along with a geometry-aware Gaussian adapter and a KL-based alignment objective, achieving state-of-the-art results on driving benchmarks.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing Driving World Models (DWMs) lack explicit 3D scene understanding, language-grounded reasoning, and controllable 4D editing capabilities. Common representations like point clouds, occupancy grids, or BEV make fine-grained alignment between textual information and underlying 3D scene structure difficult.

Method: The method consists of three key components: (1) a foundation-feature Gaussian tokenizer that directly distills Qwen/SigLIP visual-language features into 3D Gaussian primitives to build an open-vocabulary Gaussian semantic field; (2) a geometry-aware Gaussian adapter that uses importance-aware hierarchical selection with text-conditioned Perceiver-style cross-attention to aggregate dense Gaussian primitives into compact world tokens; and (3) a KL-based Gaussian-image distribution alignment objective to align Gaussian world tokens with foundation image tokens. The aligned representation supports instruction-controllable scene editing including weather-conditioned generation and dynamic vehicle manipulation.

Result: The method achieves state-of-the-art performance across scene understanding, visual grounding, planning-oriented reasoning, and controllable 4D generation tasks on broader driving benchmarks.

Conclusion: The proposed framework demonstrates that unifying 3D scene understanding, language reasoning, and 4D editing within a single Gaussian-based world model is feasible and effective, with plans to release code and datasets publicly.

Abstract: Driving World Models (DWMs) have recently advanced rapidly with generative models, yet most existing methods mainly focus on conditional scene generation and lack explicit 3D scene understanding, language-grounded reasoning, and controllable 4D editing capabilities. Moreover, commonly used point cloud, occupancy, or BEV representations make it difficult to achieve fine-grained alignment between textual information and the underlying 3D scene structure. To address these limitations, we propose a foundation-feature Gaussian driving world model that unifies scene understanding, language-grounded reasoning, controllable 4D editing, and multi-modal generation within a single framework. Specifically, we introduce a foundation-feature Gaussian tokenizer that directly distills Qwen/SigLIP visual-language features into 3D Gaussian primitives, building a compact open-vocabulary Gaussian semantic field. We further design a geometry-aware Gaussian adapter that combines importance-aware hierarchical selection with text-conditioned Perceiver-style cross-attention to aggregate dense Gaussian primitives into compact world tokens. To improve representation compatibility, we introduce a KL-based Gaussian--image distribution alignment objective that aligns Gaussian world tokens with foundation image tokens. Based on the aligned Gaussian representation, our framework further supports instruction-controllable scene editing, including weather-conditioned generation and dynamic vehicle manipulation. Extensive experiments on broader driving benchmarks demonstrate that our method achieves state-of-the-art performance across scene understanding, visual grounding, planning-oriented reasoning, and controllable 4D generation tasks. We will release the code and datasets publicly on Github.

</details>


### [153] [Convolution-Free Holistic Multivariance Decomposition Layer for Efficient Hyperspectral Image Classification Tensor Networks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.16241)
*Süha Tuna,Ülker Başar*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: The paper introduces Holistic Multivariance Decomposition (HMD), an end-to-end differentiable neural network layer for hyperspectral image classification that separates independent single-mode variations from cooperative higher-dimensional interactions via learnable matrix-valued supports. HMD-0/1/2 approximants outperform classical tensor baselines (Tucker, CP, TT) and match 2D/3D-CNN accuracy with significantly fewer parameters.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing approaches for hyperspectral image feature extraction are limited: rigid tensor decompositions fail to capture complex spatio-spectral interdependencies, while heavy convolutional neural networks are computationally expensive and parameter-heavy.

Method: Introduces the Holistic Multivariance Decomposition (HMD) framework as a novel, end-to-end differentiable neural network layer. It explicitly separates independent single-mode variations from cooperative higher-dimensional interactions using learnable, matrix-valued supports. Three variants (HMD-0, HMD-1, HMD-2) are optimized jointly with a downstream classifier via backpropagation.

Result: Comprehensive evaluations on three benchmark HS datasets show higher-level HMD layers achieve superior classification accuracy over classical learnable tensor baselines (Tucker, CP, Tensor Train). HMD-1 and HMD-2 achieve comparable generalization and training stability to 2D/3D-CNNs while requiring significantly fewer feature extractor parameters.

Conclusion: The HMD framework provides a structurally robust substitute for traditional convolution in multidimensional HS image classification, offering high parameter efficiency and stability throughout optimization.

Abstract: Feature extraction for hyperspectral image classification is conventionally addressed using rigid tensor decompositions that fail to capture complex spatio-spectral interdependencies, or heavily parameterized convolutional neural networks that are computationally expensive. To overcome these limitations, this work introduces the Holistic Multivariance Decomposition (HMD) framework as a novel, end-to-end differentiable neural network layer. By explicitly separating independent single mode variations from cooperative higher dimensional interactions via learnable, matrix valued supports, the proposed HMD-0, HMD-1 and HMD-2 approximants are optimized jointly with a downstream classifier via backpropagation. Comprehensive evaluations across three benchmark HS datasets demonstrate that the higher level HMD layers achieve superior classification accuracy compared to classical learnable tensor baselines, including Tucker, Canonical Polyadic, and Tensor Train decompositions. Furthermore, HMD-1 and HMD-2 achieve a generalization capacity and training stability comparable to standard 2D and 3D-CNNs while requiring significantly fewer feature extractor parameters. These results demonstrate that the HMD framework provides a structurally robust substitute for traditional convolution in multidimensional HS image classification, offering high parameter efficiency and stability throughout the optimization process.

</details>


### [154] [SCOUT: Semantic Concept Discovery for Open-Vocabulary Editing of face Recognition Templates](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.16251)
*Leon Todorov,Peter Rot,Peter Peer,Vitomir Štruc,Klemen Grm*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: SCOUT is an end-to-end framework for discovering and directly manipulating semantic concepts in face recognition templates using mechanistic interpretability, enabling open-vocabulary semantic editing without costly edit-re-encode pipelines.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Face recognition templates encode rich semantic information but direct semantic editing in template space remains unexplored. Existing interpretability methods rely on manual neuron inspection or predefined attribute labels, limiting scalability and semantic flexibility.

Method: SCOUT learns sparse template representations, generates semantic hypotheses for latent features from natural-language descriptions, validates their stability, and uses the resulting features as controllable semantic directions for direct template editing.

Result: Experiments with CNN, ViT, and Swin backbones show SCOUT discovers interpretable concepts beyond standard attribute labels and enables controllable, identity-aware template manipulation with negligible impact on identity matching. Edited templates can be decoded with independent inversion models for visualization.

Conclusion: SCOUT enables direct, open-vocabulary semantic editing of face recognition templates while preserving identity, overcoming the scalability and flexibility limitations of prior interpretability approaches.

Abstract: Face recognition templates are compact identity representations, yet they also encode rich semantic information about facial appearance. Prior work has shown that templates can be inverted to images or indirectly manipulated through image-editing pipelines, but direct semantic editing in template space remains largely unexplored. Existing interpretability methods for face recognition often rely on manual neuron inspection or predefined attribute labels, limiting scalability and semantic flexibility. To address this gap, we propose SCOUT (Semantic Concept Discovery for Open-VocabUlary Editing of Face Recognition Templates), an end-to-end framework for discovering and directly manipulating semantic concepts in face recognition templates using mechanistic interpretability. SCOUT learns sparse template representations, generates semantic hypotheses for latent features from natural-language descriptions, and validates their stability. The resulting features act as controllable semantic directions for direct editing, avoiding costly edit--re-encode pipelines. Experiments with face recognition models using CNN, ViT, and Swin backbones show that SCOUT discovers interpretable concepts beyond standard attribute labels and enables controllable, identity-aware template manipulation with negligible impact on identity matching. We further show that edited templates can subsequently be decoded with independent inversion models for visualization and evaluation.

</details>


### [155] [Defake-o3: From Speculative Rationales to Verifiable Evidence for Explainable AIGI Detection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.16259)
*Bowen Deng,Jiahui Zhan,Yikun Ji,Haozhen Yan,Jianfu Zhang*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: Defake-o3 is an explainable AI-generated image detector that replaces speculative rationales with verifiable evidence. It combines interactive visual search with a verifier-guided evidence alignment mechanism, supported by two new resources: GroundFake (a dataset with grounded evidence annotations) and FakeFrontier (an out-of-distribution benchmark). The method improves both detection accuracy and explanation quality.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing MLLM-based AIGI detectors produce speculative or hallucinated rationales that rely on vague artifacts, miss subtle localized flaws from the latest generators, and fail to provide visually verifiable evidence. There is a need for detectors that are not only accurate but also explainable and reliable with grounded, verifiable explanations.

Method: Defake-o3 combines interactive visual search (iteratively zooming into suspicious regions for fine-grained inspection) with verifier-guided evidence alignment. An Evidence Verifier, trained from human verification annotations, provides reinforcement learning rewards that favor grounded evidence and penalize baseless claims. The authors construct GroundFake dataset (localized bounding-box evidence, human verification, corrected reasoning trajectories, valid/invalid evidence supervision) and FakeFrontier benchmark (real images + outputs from 10 recent generators with an MLLM-based evaluation protocol).

Result: Experiments on GroundFake, FakeFrontier, and additional out-of-distribution benchmarks demonstrate that Defake-o3 improves both detection accuracy and explanation quality, producing more localized, verifiable, and persuasive evidence compared to existing methods.

Conclusion: Grounded, verifiable explanations are achievable for AIGI detection by combining iterative visual search with verifier-guided reinforcement learning, and the introduced datasets/benchmarks provide a foundation for future grounded explainable detection research.

Abstract: The rapid progress of image generation models calls for AI-generated image (AIGI) detectors that are not only accurate but also explainable and reliable. While MLLM-based detectors can provide natural language explanations, existing methods often generate speculative rationales: they rely on vague or hallucinated artifacts, miss subtle localized flaws from the latest generators, and fail to provide evidence that can be visually verified. We present Defake-o3, an explainable AIGI detector that moves from speculative rationales to verifiable evidence. It combines interactive visual search with verifier-guided evidence alignment: the model iteratively zooms into suspicious regions to inspect fine-grained details, while an Evidence Verifier, trained from human verification annotations, provides reinforcement learning rewards that favor grounded evidence and penalize baseless claims. To support this objective, we construct GroundFake, a dataset designed for grounded explainable detection, with localized bounding-box evidence, human verification based on visual grounding and artifact specificity, corrected reasoning trajectories, and valid/invalid evidence supervision. We further introduce FakeFrontier, an out-of-distribution benchmark built from real images and outputs of 10 recent generators, together with an MLLM-based protocol for evaluating evidence quality and persuasiveness. Experiments on GroundFake, FakeFrontier, and additional out-of-distribution benchmarks show that Defake-o3 improves both detection accuracy and explanation quality, producing more localized, verifiable, and persuasive evidence.

</details>


### [156] [Seeing Before Answering: Training-Free Visual Layer Profiling for Vision-Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.16263)
*Ruchen Liu,Yi Yang,Yiming Xu,Michael Ying Yang,Monika Sester,Bodo Rosenhahn*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: The paper challenges the default practice of using a fixed late vision layer (penultimate) in LLaVA-style VLMs, showing it is sub-optimal in 13 of 14 model-task pairs. The authors propose predicting the best visual layer without expensive exhaustive inference, using Visual Dataset Entropy (VDE) computed from 100 unlabeled samples pre-projector, which tracks layer-wise accuracy and identifies top candidate layers, while Gromov-Wasserstein distance works as an alignment diagnostic rather than a layer selector.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The default practice of using a fixed (penultimate) visual layer in LLaVA-style VLMs is sub-optimal across most tasks and backbones, yet exhaustive layer-wise search is prohibitively expensive. The authors seek a training-free, interpretable method to predict which visual layer is best for a given task and backbone.

Method: The authors (1) empirically demonstrate the fragility of the default layer choice across 2 VLMs and 7 benchmarks; (2) transfer two existing geometric measures to layer selection: Visual Dataset Entropy (VDE), a matrix-based entropy computed over sample-level visual embeddings, and Gromov-Wasserstein (GW) distance as a visual-language alignment signal; (3) profile both sides of the multimodal projector (pre- and post-projection) to account for the frozen vision tower and trained projector.

Result: VDE transfers effectively: pre-projector VDE from just 100 unlabeled samples tracks layer-wise accuracy, and its top-ranked layers cover the oracle best layer on every task for SigLIP-based LLaVA-Video, while providing region-level guidance for CLIP-based Video-LLaVA. GW does not transfer as a selector—it flattens after projection and serves best as an alignment diagnostic. The projector reshapes visual geometry but does not erase the performance-relevant trend, making pre-projector VDE the stronger signal.

Conclusion: VDE provides an interpretable, training-free policy that narrows the visual-layer search space to a handful of candidates for limited downstream verification, avoiding exhaustive layer-wise inference. Post-projector analysis reveals the projector's reshaping effects but confirms pre-projector VDE remains the most reliable layer selector.

Abstract: LLaVA-style Vision-Language Models (VLMs) pass visual tokens from a fixed late layer of the vision backbone, typically the penultimate one, to the language model. We first show that this hidden convention is fragile: across 2 VLMs and 7 image and video benchmarks, the default layer is sub-optimal in 13 of 14 model-task pairs, and the best layer shifts with both task and visual backbone. Finding that layer by exhaustive layer-wise inference is prohibitively expensive, and no better fixed default exists. We therefore ask whether layer usefulness can instead be predicted from representation geometry. We study matrix-based entropy, introduced for unimodal layer analysis, which we compute over sample-level visual embeddings as Visual Dataset Entropy (VDE); and Gromov-Wasserstein (GW) distance, introduced for encoder-level VLM model selection, which we repurpose as a layer-wise visual--language alignment signal. Transferring these to LLaVA-based models is not obvious a priori: the vision tower is frozen while the multimodal projector is trained, so we profile both sides of the projector. We find that VDE transfers, and GW does not. Computed from 100 unlabeled task samples without downstream inference, pre-projector VDE tracks layer-wise accuracy and its top-ranked layers cover the oracle best layer on every task for the SigLIP-based LLaVA-Video, while giving region-level guidance for the CLIP-based Video-LLaVA. Post-projector profiles show that the projector reshapes visual geometry but does not erase the performance-relevant trend, leaving $\mathrm{VDE}_{\mathrm{pre}}$ the stronger signal. GW instead flattens after projection and is best read as an alignment diagnostic rather than a selector. VDE thus offers an interpretable, training-free policy that narrows the visual-layer search to a handful of candidates for limited downstream verification.

</details>


### [157] [CoM$^3$eT: A foundation model for medical image analysis through federated, multidimensional context integration](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.16268)
*J. Raphael Schäfer,Kai Geissler,Till Nicke,Chiara Tappermann,Karoline Heber,Eike Petersen,Habib Mergan,Lars Ole Schwen,Nick Weiss,Annika Gerken,Jan Hendrik Moltz,Tom Bisson,Isil Dogan O,Tim-Rasmus Kiehl,Norman Zerbe,Sefer Elezkurtaj,Robin S. Mayer,Nadine Flinner,Peter Wild,Isabel Dahm,Felix Peisen,Heinrich von Busch,Robert Grimm,Sebastian Arndt,Lisa Siegler,Matthias Stefan May,Antje Prasse,Natalia Artysh,Fabian Kiessling,Johannes Lotz*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: CoM³eT is a medical vision foundation model that unifies pathology and radiology, sparse and dense predictions, and 2D/3D inputs via multidimensional context modeling with attention, outperforming other medical foundation models across diverse datasets and tasks, while enabling parameter-efficient adaptation and federated learning with consumer-grade hardware.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing medical foundation models are limited to single specialties (e.g., pathology or radiology) and single output types (sparse like classification or dense like segmentation), lacking a unified approach that can handle multiple modalities, dimensions, and task types simultaneously.

Method: CoM³eT uses co-representation multidimensional multitask architecture with attention mechanisms to model multidimensional context, unifying pathology and radiology, sparse and dense prediction tasks, and both 2D and 3D (higher-dimensional) inputs within a single medical vision foundation model.

Result: CoM³eT outperformed other medical foundation models in an open competition spanning 12 datasets (five tomographic, four whole-specimen, three 2D), covering sparse/dense prediction tasks and report generation. It achieved comparable performance to full fine-tuning while training fewer than 2.5% of parameters, and achieved pooled-data-comparable performance in federated learning across hospitals using internet connections and consumer-grade hardware.

Conclusion: A unified medical vision foundation model spanning specialties, dimensions, and task types is feasible and effective, democratizing medical AI research by enabling high performance with minimal training parameters and consumer-grade hardware, while supporting privacy-preserving federated learning across institutions.

Abstract: Medical foundation models improve generalization when training AI models with limited labeled data, but remain confined to a single specialty, such as pathology or radiology, and to either sparse or dense outputs, such as classification or segmentation. Here, we present CoM$^3$eT (Co-representation Multidimensional Multitask Medical Transformer), a medical vision foundation model that unifies pathology and radiology, sparse and dense predictions, and two- and higher-dimensional inputs by modeling multidimensional context with attention. CoM$^3$eT outperformed other medical foundation models in an open competition spanning five tomographic, four whole-specimen, and three two-dimensional datasets, covering sparse and dense prediction tasks as well as report generation. When adapted across diverse clinical applications, training fewer than 2.5% of parameters achieved performance comparable to full fine-tuning, enabling research without access to high-performance GPU clusters. Applied to federated learning across hospitals, this approach achieved performance comparable to pooled-data training over internet connections and with consumer-grade hardware.

</details>


### [158] [TransAnyText: Translating Arbitrary Text in E-commerce Images via Structured Visual Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.16284)
*Xiaoan Liu,Lichen Ma,Zipeng Guo,Yu He,Xiaoyan Su,Shaojie Guo,Hao Yang,Jingling Fu,Xiaolong Fu,Zhen Chen,Yu Guo,Fei Wang,Xinyi Liu,Yongjun Zhang,Ke Zhang,Junshi Huang*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: TransAnyText is a structured visual code framework for cross-border e-commerce image translation that reformulates text translation as generating renderable HTML patches, decoupling semantic generation (via VLM) from pixel rendering (via diffusion model), with a three-stage post-training pipeline (SFT, privilege-gap weighted self-distillation, and RLVR) achieving competitive results against existing systems.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing methods for e-commerce image translation fail to simultaneously achieve accurate translation, faithful visual identity preservation, and easy-to-edit outputs. This motivates the need for a new framework that addresses all three challenges together.

Method: The framework reformulates image text translation as generating renderable HTML patches. It decouples semantic generation from pixel rendering: a vision-language model handles visual understanding, translation, and structured generation, while a diffusion model performs background inpainting and pixel refinement, followed by deterministic rendering. A three-stage post-training pipeline is used: SFT for image-to-code mapping, privilege-gap weighted self-distillation (PWSD) for style/layout token learning, and RLVR for task-level optimization. A multilingual dataset (TransAnyDataset) and benchmark (TransAnyBench) are introduced.

Result: Extensive experiments demonstrate competitive performance against cascaded pipelines, open-source end-to-end models, and closed-source image editing systems, providing an effective, controllable, and editable solution.

Conclusion: TransAnyText offers a novel structured visual code approach that achieves accurate translation, visual identity preservation, and editability simultaneously for e-commerce image translation.

Abstract: Cross-border e-commerce image translation is essential for global retail, where product images, banners, and detail pages need to be produced in different languages. Existing methods struggle to achieve accurate translation, faithful visual identity preservation, and easy-to-edit outputs, simultaneously. To address these challenges, we introduce TransAnyText, a structured visual code framework that reformulates image text translation as generating renderable HTML patches from source images and target languages. Our framework decouples semantic generation from pixel rendering: a vision-language model (VLM) handles visual understanding, cross-lingual translation, and structured visual generation, while a diffusion model performs background inpainting and pixel-level refinement, followed by deterministic rendering to synthesize the final image. Based on this formulation, we develop a three-stage post-training framework, where supervised fine-tuning (SFT) establishes the image-to-code mapping, privilege-gap weighted self-distillation (PWSD) improves the learning of style and layout tokens, and reinforcement learning with verifiable rewards (RLVR) further optimizes task-level performance. We further introduce TransAnyDataset and TransAnyBench, a multilingual dataset and benchmark for e-commerce image translation. Extensive experiments demonstrate competitive performance against cascaded pipelines, open-source end-to-end models, and closed-source image editing systems, providing an effective, controllable, and editable solution for cross-border e-commerce image translation.

</details>


### [159] [Audio-Visual Segmentation via Depth-Guided Collaborative Modeling](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.16285)
*Zhaojin Fu,Yuyang Hong,Qi Yang,Zili Wang,Kun Ding,Shiming Xiang,Bin Fan*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: DGCM-AVS is a tri-modal audio-visual segmentation framework that incorporates estimated depth as a spatial structural cue, using a Depth-Aware Dynamic Modulator and Depth-Guided Progressive Fusion to improve cross-modal alignment. It achieves 10.2% relative improvement in M_J and 8.7% in M_F on the AVSS dataset compared to state-of-the-art methods.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing AVS methods do not explicitly model geometric cues such as relative distance and occlusion, which limits the robustness of cross-modal alignment. Since human perception naturally integrates spatial structure with audio-visual evidence, the authors are motivated to incorporate depth information as a spatial structural cue.

Method: The paper proposes DGCM-AVS, a tri-modal framework jointly modeling audio, visual, and depth information. It includes two key components: (1) a Depth-Aware Dynamic Modulator to improve separation of adjacent objects while preserving intra-object feature consistency, and (2) a Depth-Guided Progressive Fusion mechanism that uses depth as an intermediate bridge to progressively align audio cues with visual features.

Result: DGCM-AVS achieves relative improvements of 10.2% in M_J and 8.7% in M_F on the AVSS dataset compared to state-of-the-art methods.

Conclusion: The study highlights depth as a promising yet underexplored modality for audio-visual segmentation and may encourage further research in this direction.

Abstract: Audio-Visual Segmentation (AVS) is a fundamental task in multimodal perception that performs pixel-level segmentation of sounding objects in videos by leveraging both visual and audio cues. It has broad applications in video understanding, human-computer interaction, and autonomous driving. However, most existing AVS methods do not explicitly model geometric cues such as relative distance and occlusion, thereby limiting the robustness of cross-modal alignment. In human perception, spatial structure is naturally integrated with audio-visual evidence to accurately localize sounding objects. Motivated by this, we incorporate estimated depth as a spatial structural cue for AVS and propose DGCM-AVS, a tri-modal framework that jointly models audio, visual, and depth information. Specifically, we design a Depth-Aware Dynamic Modulator to improve the separation of adjacent objects while preserving intra-object feature consistency. Furthermore, we propose Depth-Guided Progressive Fusion, which uses depth as an intermediate bridge to progressively align audio cues with visual features. Compared to state-of-the-art methods, DGCM-AVS achieves relative improvements of 10.2 percent in M_J and 8.7 percent in M_F on the AVSS dataset. We believe our study highlights depth as a promising yet underexplored modality for AVS and may encourage further research in this direction.

</details>


### [160] [PosterText: Towards Unified Visual Text Generation and Editing for E-commerce Poster](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.16289)
*Xiaoan Liu,Lichen Ma,Zipeng Guo,Yu He,Xiaoyan Su,Shaojie Guo,Jingling Fu,Xiaolong Fu,Hao Yang,Tongxuan Liu,Yu Guo,Fei Wang,Xinyi Liu,Yongjun Zhang,Junshi Huang*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: PosterText is a unified model for both e-commerce poster generation and editing, treating text patches as atomic units to support four operations (generation, addition, deletion, modification) with optional reference-guided style control, trained via a four-stage curriculum.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing e-commerce poster design methods either focus solely on end-to-end generation or follow rigid multi-stage pipelines, lacking the capability for flexible and precise editing of existing poster designs.

Method: Introduces Text Patch Generation and Editing (TPGE) as a unified task formulation, and proposes PosterText trained with a four-stage curriculum: text rendering pretraining, instruction-following training, reinforcement learning for preference alignment, and spatial guidance self-distillation for execution refinement. A large-scale dataset with patch-level annotations and a comprehensive benchmark are also constructed.

Result: Extensive experiments demonstrate that PosterText achieves competitive performance against existing generation and editing approaches, validating the effectiveness of the proposed framework.

Conclusion: The unified framework successfully bridges poster generation and editing capabilities, providing a flexible solution for automated e-commerce poster design.

Abstract: Automated e-commerce poster design requires both high-quality poster generation and flexible editing of existing designs. However, most existing methods either target end-to-end poster generation or follow multi-stage design pipelines, with limited capability for flexible and precise editing of existing posters.
