*Hieu D. Pham,Dang P. M. Cao,Thanh Trung Huynh*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: TCSR-Monitor is a post-hoc failure-monitoring framework for surgical segmentation that combines confidence with shape, temporal-consistency, and image-quality cues to detect confident failures under acquisition degradation, outperforming confidence-only baselines but still suffering from false alarms and transfer limitations.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Surgical segmentation networks can fail silently under acquisition degradation, producing wrong predictions with high confidence. Existing deployment-time monitors rely primarily on uncertainty estimates and therefore miss these confident failures, motivating a need for complementary observable signals beyond confidence alone.

Method: TCSR-Monitor wraps a frozen segmentation model as a post-hoc monitor, requiring no model internals and no ground truth at deployment. It combines confidence with observable shape, temporal-consistency, and image-quality cues. A validation protocol assesses alarm credibility under distribution shift, and Mondrian conformal calibration balances miss-rates across degradation severities.

Result: On EndoVis 2017 with leave-one-corruption-out evaluation, TCSR-Monitor generalizes to unseen acquisition degradations and substantially outperforms confidence-based baselines. A circularity control confirms it predicts segmentation failure rather than merely detecting corrupted images. However, a single global threshold produces false alarms on up to 40% of correctly segmented frames at moderate corruption. Zero-shot transfer to SAM2 shows feature portability, though entropy outperforms the transferred monitor at both evaluated thresholds.

Conclusion: Reliable monitoring under acquisition degradation benefits from complementary observable signals beyond confidence alone, but substantial false-alarm and transfer limitations remain, indicating room for further improvement.

Abstract: Surgical segmentation networks can fail silently under acquisition degradation: predicted masks may be wrong even when model confidence remains high. Existing deployment-time monitors rely primarily on uncertainty estimates and can therefore miss confident failures. We present TCSR-Monitor (Temporal Conformal Surgical Risk Monitor), a post-hoc failure-monitoring framework that combines confidence with observable shape, temporal-consistency, and image-quality cues. TCSR-Monitor wraps a frozen segmentation model, requires no model internals, and operates without ground truth at deployment. We also introduce a validation protocol to assess whether alarms remain credible under distribution shift. On EndoVis 2017, leave-one-corruption-out evaluation shows that TCSR-Monitor generalizes to unseen acquisition degradations and substantially outperforms confidence-based baselines. A circularity control confirms that it predicts segmentation failure rather than simply detecting corrupted images. Mondrian conformal calibration balances miss-rates across degradation severities, but a single global threshold still produces false alarms on up to 40% of correctly segmented frames at moderate corruption. Zero-shot transfer to SAM2 demonstrates feature portability, although entropy outperforms the transferred monitor at both evaluated thresholds. Overall, reliable monitoring under acquisition degradation benefits from complementary observable signals beyond confidence alone, but substantial false-alarm and transfer limitations remain.

</details>


### [207] [Binarized High-Efficiency RAW Video Restoration and Beyond](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.16756)
*Tianyu Zhu,Ying Fu,Hesong Li,Gengchen Zhang,Xin Yuan,Yulun Zhang*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: BinRVR is a binarized RAW video restoration framework that reduces computation and parameters by ~96% with only ~4% performance degradation, using a Binarized Information Interaction Module (BIIM) for spatial-temporal modeling and a Distribution-Aware Binarized Convolution (DAB-Conv) to mitigate quantization errors, supporting multi-bit quantization for flexible accuracy-efficiency trade-offs.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: While binary neural networks (BNNs) enable efficient lightweight deployment for image enhancement, they suffer from deficiencies in modeling temporal coherence and activation value distributions when applied to video scenarios, limiting their effectiveness for RAW video restoration.

Method: The framework introduces two key components: (1) a Binarized Information Interaction Module (BIIM) that jointly models spatial and temporal information efficiently, and (2) a Distribution-Aware Binarized Convolution (DAB-Conv) that uses statistics of full-precision activations to reduce quantization errors. The framework also supports multi-bit quantization for flexible accuracy-efficiency trade-offs.

Result: Extensive experiments show BinRVR achieves competitive performance compared with state-of-the-art binarized methods on RAW video restoration tasks (low-light enhancement, denoising, deblurring, super-resolution). It reduces computation/parameters by ~96% with only ~4% performance degradation, and shows potential on downstream tasks including object detection and monocular depth estimation.

Conclusion: BinRVR effectively addresses the limitations of BNNs in video scenarios, delivering efficient and competitive RAW video restoration with flexible quantization options, and demonstrates potential for downstream video applications.

Abstract: RAW video restoration is fundamental to high-quality low-level perception and serves as the basis for a wide range of downstream vision applications. While binary neural networks (BNNs) enable efficient lightweight deployment for image enhancement, their deficiencies in modeling temporal coherence and activation value distributions hinder their effectiveness when applied to video scenarios. In this paper, we propose BinRVR, a binarized RAW video restoration framework that reduces computation and parameters by approximately 96% while incurring only about 4% performance degradation. Specifically, we present a Binarized Information Interaction Module (BIIM) to jointly model spatial and temporal information in an efficient and unified manner. Moreover, we develop a Distribution-Aware Binarized Convolution (DAB-Conv) that leverages the statistics of full-precision activations to mitigate quantization errors. The proposed framework further supports multi-bit quantization, enabling flexible accuracy-efficiency trade-offs across different hardware constraints. Extensive experiments demonstrate that our BinRVR achieves competitive performance compared with state-of-the-art binarized methods on RAW video restoration tasks, including low-light enhancement, denoising, deblurring, and super-resolution. We further explore the potential of our method on downstream video applications, including object detection and monocular depth estimation.

</details>


### [208] [TRACE-Bench: Decomposing and Diagnosing Multi-Reference Image Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.16765)
*Haoran Wang,Chaofan Ma,Ran Yi,Lizhuang Ma*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: TRACE-Bench is a capability-oriented benchmark for multi-reference image generation, formalizing multi-reference tasks into four atomic operators (Anchor, Disentangle, Apply, Compose) and evaluating 9 models across ~1,600 cases, revealing that disentanglement and attribute binding are the primary bottlenecks rather than scene-level composition.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing benchmarks for multi-reference image generation are organized around predefined task types (e.g., "subject composition"), which poorly suit the combinatorial setting, leading to fragmented coverage, uncontrolled complexity, and little diagnostic value.

Method: Formalize four atomic operators: Anchor (f), Disentangle (g), Apply (&#8853;), and Compose (C). Any multi-reference prompt is represented as a compositional formula over these operators, with structural complexity measured by the number of operator slots. Build TRACE-Bench with ~1,600 cases across slot counts 1-8, from 631 formula templates and ~4,000 reference images. Use an operator-aligned evaluation protocol and diagnostic tree analysis for failure localization.

Result: Evaluating 9 leading models reveals the primary bottleneck lies in disentanglement (g) and attribute binding (&#8853;) rather than scene-level composition (C), with the best model scoring only 0.74 on attribute fidelity.

Conclusion: The capability-oriented, operator-based formulation provides diagnostic insights invisible to holistic scoring, exposing specific capability weaknesses in current models that should guide future improvements.

Abstract: Despite recent advances in unified multimodal models for multi-reference image generation, existing benchmarks remain organized around predefined task types (e.g., "subject composition"), which are ill-suited to this combinatorial setting and lead to fragmented coverage, uncontrolled complexity, and little diagnostic value. Recognizing that diverse multi-reference tasks share a common set of atomic operations, we adopt a capability-oriented perspective and formalize four operators: Anchor ($f$), Disentangle ($g$), Apply ($\oplus$), and Compose ($C$). Any multi-reference prompt can then be represented as a compositional formula over these operators, whose structural complexity is quantified by the number of operator slots. Building on this formulation, we construct TRACE-Bench, comprising approximately 1,600 evaluation cases across slot counts 1--8, built from 631 formula templates and around 4,000 reference images spanning diverse artistic styles and real-world subjects. The formula structure directly drives an operator-aligned evaluation protocol for per-capability scoring and a diagnostic tree analysis for recursive failure localization. Evaluating 9 leading models reveals insights invisible to holistic scoring: the primary bottleneck lies in disentanglement ($g$) and attribute binding ($\oplus$) rather than scene-level composition ($C$), with even the best model scoring only 0.74 on attribute fidelity. Project page: https://amuseum-whr.github.io/TraceBench

</details>


### [209] [Calibration-Free Vehicle Speed Estimation: A Monocular Keypoint-Template Approach](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.16785)
*Gaofeng Su,Keya Li,Raja Sengupta,Kara M. Kockelman*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: The paper proposes a calibration-free framework for estimating vehicle speeds from monocular videos using a 36-keypoint vehicle template and a frame-updated homography matrix. It achieves reliable speed estimation on VS13 and BrnoCompSpeed datasets (MAEs of 15.0% and 9.7%, improving to 11.7% and 7.6% after outlier trimming), enabling low-cost speed enforcement via portable devices like dashcams.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing vision-based vehicle speed estimation methods rely on roadway features, camera calibration, or reference objects, limiting their applicability in diverse real-world settings. The goal is to enable low-cost, portable, and citizen-based speed enforcement without such dependencies.

Method: A YOLO-based keypoint detection module (36-keypoint vehicle template) is trained on diverse datasets. A homography matrix is updated at each frame. Two estimation strategies are compared: keypoint-only tracking and warped optical flow with dense spatial aggregation. Speeds are estimated by projecting displacements into metric space using the homography.

Result: Validation on over 400 video clips (speeds 30-100 mph) from roadside and overhead datasets shows warped optical flow achieves MAEs of 15.0% and 9.7% on VS13 and BrnoCompSpeed, with 77.9% and 93.1% within +/-20% error. After 10% trimming, MAEs improve to 11.7% and 7.6%, with accuracy rising to 85.3% and 95.4%.

Conclusion: The calibration-free framework addresses key limitations of existing vision-based approaches, enabling efficient and low-cost speed enforcement using portable devices such as dashcams and smartphones, thus supporting citizen-based traffic safety enforcement programs.

Abstract: This paper proposes a calibration-free framework for reliably and effectively estimating vehicle speeds from monocular videos, without relying on roadway features, camera calibration, or roadway-feature-based reference objects. The proposed framework estimates vehicle speeds using a 36-keypoint vehicle template and a homography matrix updated at each frame. A YOLO-based keypoint detection module is trained on diverse datasets, and two estimation strategies are compared: keypoint-only tracking and warped optical flow with dense spatial aggregation. Speed is estimated by projecting displacements into metric space using the homography, with validation conducted on over 400 video clips from roadside and overhead datasets, covering speeds from 30 to 100 mph. The method achieves reliable speed estimation on the VS13 and BrnoCompSpeed datasets, with the warped optical flow method delivering MAEs of 15.0% and 9.7%, respectively, and 77.9% and 93.1% of estimates falling within +/-20% error. After applying a 10% trim to remove edge-of-frame outliers, performance improves to MAEs of 11.7% and 7.6%, with within-+/-20% accuracy increasing to 85.3% and 95.4%. This work addresses key limitations of existing vision-based approaches and enables low-cost and efficient speed enforcement using portable devices such as dashcams and smartphones, thereby supporting citizen-based enforcement programs for traffic safety.

</details>


### [210] [Revisiting Classifier-Free Guidance Methods in Latent Diffusion Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.16786)
*Artem Sergievskii,Artyom Turevich,Sergey Kastryulin*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: This paper re-evaluates eight training-free inference-time quality-enhancement methods (conceptually rooted in Classifier-Free Guidance) on two open-weight rectified-flow transformer models using three compositional-alignment benchmarks. Results show that no method consistently outperforms CFG, with APG achieving nominal best scores that often fall within evaluation uncertainty, and attention-perturbation methods yielding mixed results that vary by model.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing inference-time enhancement methods were largely developed on older U-Net diffusion models and validated with metrics that only assess image quality in isolation, ignoring compositional alignment and semantic correspondence between generated images and their text prompts. This creates a gap in understanding how these methods perform on modern transformer-based diffusion models under compositional benchmarks.

Method: The authors re-evaluate eight training-free enhancement methods (conceptually related to Classifier-Free Guidance) under a fixed per-model protocol on two open-weight rectified-flow transformers, using three compositional-alignment benchmarks that measure both image quality and semantic correspondence with text prompts.

Result: No method consistently outperforms CFG across measured criteria. APG achieves several nominal best scores, but the gains usually fall within estimated evaluation uncertainty. Attention-perturbation methods show isolated gains on SD3.5 Medium but more frequent degradations on FLUX.2 [klein] 4B Base, while CFG remains a competitive, lower-cost baseline.

Conclusion: CFG remains a competitive and computationally cheaper baseline, suggesting that the benefits of more complex inference-time enhancement methods may not generalize from older U-Net architectures to modern rectified-flow transformers when compositional alignment is taken into account.

Abstract: Inference-time quality-enhancement methods are an effective and widely adopted means of improving diffusion models without expensive retraining. We study a family of training-free techniques conceptually rooted in Classifier-Free Guidance (CFG), most of which were originally proposed on older U-Net diffusion models and validated using metrics that assess image quality in isolation, without accounting for compositional alignment or semantic correspondence between the generated image and its associated text prompt. We re-evaluate eight such methods on two open-weight rectified-flow transformers under a fixed per-model protocol and three compositional-alignment benchmarks. No method consistently improves on CFG across the measured criteria. APG obtains several nominal best scores, but the corresponding gains often remain within the estimated evaluation uncertainty. Attention-perturbation methods provide isolated gains on SD3.5 Medium and more frequent degradations on FLUX.2 [klein] 4B Base, while CFG remains a competitive lower-cost baseline.

</details>


### [211] [Steering the Flow: Inverting Face Recognition Models via Gradient-Guided Flow Matching](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.16791)
*Ye Lu,Shen Wang,Zhaoyang Zhang,Yihan Yan,Li Liu,Runze Liu,Fanghui Sun*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: SFMI is a two-stage white-box model inversion attack that reformulates inversion as a trajectory-steering task using a pre-trained Flow Matching prior and a Progressive Guidance Scheduler to inject time-dependent target-specific gradients, achieving state-of-the-art attack success and visual fidelity on face recognition models.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing Model Inversion Attacks rely on indirect or highly stochastic guidance, making it difficult to stably optimize generation trajectories toward target facial images.

Method: SFMI consists of two stages: (1) pre-training a generic unconditional Flow Matching model to encode the manifold of human faces as a robust prior, and (2) attacking with a Progressive Guidance Scheduler (PGS) that injects time-dependent target-specific gradients during sampling by backpropagating through the target model to steer the generative flow from random noise toward high-density regions of the target class.

Result: Under an identity-disjoint cross-evaluation setting using the CelebA dataset, SFMI achieves ACC of 0.9248, FID of 22.61, and LPIPS of 0.3874 on the ArcFace target, with competitive state-of-the-art performance across multiple target models.

Conclusion: SFMI demonstrates that reformulating model inversion as a trajectory-steering task with flow matching priors and progressive guidance yields competitive state-of-the-art attack success and visual fidelity under the white-box protocol.

Abstract: Model Inversion Attacks (MIAs) aim to reconstruct representative training samples of target identities from face recognition models, exposing critical security vulnerabilities. Existing methods typically rely on indirect guidance or highly stochastic guidance, making it difficult to stably optimize generation trajectories toward target facial images. In this paper, we propose Steering Flow Model Inversion (SFMI), a novel two-stage white-box model inversion method that reformulates inversion as a trajectory-steering task. Specifically, Step I, Learning a Generic Flow Matching Prior, pre-trains a generic unconditional Flow Matching model to encode the manifold of human faces as a robust prior. Step II, Attacking with Progressive Guidance Scheduler (PGS), injects time-dependent target-specific gradients during sampling. By backpropagating through the target model to obtain gradients from intermediate generated states, PGS progressively injects adaptive guidance signals into the vector field. This process effectively steers the current generative flow from random noise toward the high-density regions of the target class. Under an identity-disjoint cross-evaluation setting using the CelebA dataset, SFMI achieves an ACC of 0.9248, an FID of 22.61, and an LPIPS of 0.3874 on the ArcFace target. Extensive experiments on multiple target models demonstrate that SFMI achieves competitive state-of-the-art performance in attack success and visual fidelity under the evaluated white-box protocol.

</details>


### [212] [PixRestore: Unified Image Restoration via Pixel Diffusion Transformer](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.16793)
*Lingchen Sun,Rongyuan Wu,Xiangtao Kong,Jixin Zhao,Qiaosi Yi,Yujing Sun,Shuaizheng Liu,Zhengqiang Zhang,Lei Zhang*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: PixRestore is a VAE-free pixel-space Diffusion Transformer (DiT) for unified image restoration, trained from scratch with flow matching on patchified pixels, using DINO feature similarity to adapt conditioning across degradations, and distilled into a one-step generator via DINO-based adversarial objectives—achieving state-of-the-art fidelity, perceptual quality, and robustness with only ~50M parameters.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Recent UIR methods rely on latent T2I diffusion models, but their VAE discards restoration-sensitive details and open-ended synthesis priors introduce content-inconsistent artifacts. The authors aim to overcome these limitations with a pixel-space design that preserves fine details without pretrained T2I dependencies.

Method: PixRestore is a pixel-space Diffusion Transformer trained from scratch via flow matching on patchified pixels. It computes LQ-HQ DINO feature similarity to estimate reliability of layer features: reliable layers are fused as dense conditioning, while unreliable layers receive stronger HQ-feature supervision. It is further finetuned into a one-step generator using DINO-based adversarial objectives.

Result: With ~50M parameters and single-step inference, PixRestore achieves the best overall fidelity, perceptual quality, and robustness to degradations among competing UIR models, while being substantially more efficient. Larger variants further boost performance, showing scalability of the pixel-space design.

Conclusion: The pixel-space DiT approach with reliability-aware feature conditioning and single-step distillation provides an efficient yet high-performing alternative to latent T2I-based UIR, with strong scalability and comprehensive benchmark performance.

Abstract: Unified image restoration (UIR) aims to recover high-quality (HQ) content from low-quality (LQ) images with different degradations using a single model. Most recent methods adapt large pretrained text-to-image (T2I) latent diffusion models for their strong capacity and generative priors. However, the variational autoencoder (VAE) in latent T2I models may discard restoration-sensitive details, while the open-ended synthesis prior can introduce content-inconsistent artifacts. We present PixRestore, a VAE-free pixel-space Diffusion Transformer (DiT) for UIR, where the diffusion backbone is trained entirely from scratch, without relying on T2I pretraining. PixRestore performs flow matching directly on patchified pixels, preserving fine-grained details while keeping the token sequence tractable. To adapt to different degradations, PixRestore learns to predict the reliability of layer features using LQ--HQ DINO feature similarity. Features from more reliable layers are fused as dense conditioning, while less reliable layers receive stronger HQ-feature supervision to encourage degradation removal. We train PixRestore on a large-scale corpus of diverse scenes and degradations, and further finetune it into a one-step generator using DINO-based adversarial objectives for efficient inference. Experiments on public benchmarks and real-world test sets show that, with only about 50M parameters and single-step inference, PixRestore achieves the best overall fidelity, perceptual quality, and robustness to degradations among competing UIR models while being far more efficient. Larger PixRestore variants can further boost performance, demonstrating the scalability of our pixel-space design. Code and the curated benchmark can be found at https://github.com/csslc/PixRestore.

</details>


### [213] [Diagnosing Dense Same-Class Attribute Misbinding in Large Vision-Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.16805)
*Yuanzhi Xu,Qian Gao,Jun Fan,Guohui Ding,Zhenyu Yang,Yuteng Xiao,Sixue Lin*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: This paper introduces Dense Same-Class Attribute Misbinding (DSCAM), a failure mode where vision-language models assign an attribute to the wrong instance among visually similar same-class entities. The authors present InstaBind-Lite, a benchmark of 524 images with 529 curated groups, 1773 boxed instances, and 9580 questions that can deterministically detect such misbinding through source-instance annotations. Experiments across seven models reveal 7.55-19.84% misbinding rates hidden by aggregate accuracy, with ~80% of transferred attributes originating from adjacent instances, and show that localization/intervention methods only partially help.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing VQA accuracy and object-hallucination metrics fail to detect when a model correctly recognizes both an object and an attribute but wrongly attributes one to another instance of the same class. This blind spot is not captured by current evaluation protocols.

Method: The authors formalize DSCAM, build InstaBind-Lite consisting of 529 curated same-class groups with boxed instances, distinguish source-instance annotations to separate unsupported generation and recognition failure from attribute transfer, and design binding-specific metrics (transfer frequency, adjacency, ordinal distance, intervention effects) with four complementary question levels.

Result: Open-source models average 19.84% Misbinding Rate and API models 7.55%, both hidden by aggregate accuracy. Among identifiable transfers, 80.70% (open-source) and 81.51% (API) originate from adjacent instances. Localization and instance-first interventions help only selected models, not universally.

Conclusion: InstaBind-Lite converts previously undifferentiated wrong answers into source-identifiable failure categories, enabling evaluation of a critical reliability dimension: whether a model knows not just what is visible but which instance owns each attribute—a capability conventional benchmarks cannot assess.

Abstract: Large vision-language models can recognize the objects and attributes in a crowded scene yet assign an attribute to the wrong same-class instance. Generic visual-question-answering accuracy marks the response as wrong, while object-hallucination metrics may regard both the object and attribute as image-supported; neither reveals the transfer. This study formalizes this blind spot as Dense Same-Class Attribute Misbinding (DSCAM) and presents InstaBind-Lite, a controlled benchmark that makes it directly measurable. Its 524 images contain 529 curated groups of 3-6 same-class entities, 1773 boxed instances, ordered neighbors, distinguishable color-like attributes, and four complementary question levels, yielding 9580 deterministically evaluated questions. Unlike existing protocols, source-instance annotations separate unsupported generation and recognition failure from an attribute copied from another visible entity. Binding-specific metrics further quantify transfer frequency, adjacency, ordinal distance, and intervention effects. Across five open-source and two commercial/API models, the open-source systems average 19.84% Misbinding Rate and the API systems 7.55%; these errors are hidden by aggregate accuracy. Among identifiable transfers, 80.70% and 81.51%, respectively, originate from adjacent instances. Localization and instance-first interventions help selected models but are not universal remedies. InstaBind-Lite therefore turns previously undifferentiated wrong answers into source-identifiable failure categories and tests a reliability dimension that conventional benchmarks cannot determine: whether a model knows not only what is visible, but which instance owns each attribute.

</details>


### [214] [Unsupervised Learning of Cell Instances with Generative Routing Pyramids](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.16810)
*Ziwen Liu,Martin Weigert*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: This paper introduces an unsupervised method for cell instance segmentation and phenotypic classification from unlabeled microscopy images, using a coarse-to-fine routing pyramid that reconstructs images via spatially sparse latent sources. Pixel-to-latent associations produce instance masks while latents encode morphology, achieving competitive results across diverse cell types and imaging modalities.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Traditional machine learning workflows for microscopy image analysis require supervised detection/segmentation followed by feature extraction, which demands manual annotations and treats segmentation and representation as separate stages. There is a need for an unsupervised approach that unifies these tasks without labeled data.

Method: The method uses a coarse-to-fine routing pyramid to reconstruct each image by associating pixels with spatially sparse latent sources. These pixel-to-latent associations generate instance masks, while the latent codes themselves encode cell morphology, enabling both segmentation and phenotypic classification in a single unsupervised framework.

Result: The method demonstrates competitive performance in instance segmentation across diverse cell morphologies and imaging modalities, and supports generative modeling of cellular phenotypes under perturbations. Code and checkpoints are publicly released.

Conclusion: The proposed unsupervised routing-pyramid approach successfully unifies cell instance segmentation and morphological representation learning without manual annotations, offering a competitive alternative to supervised pipelines across diverse imaging conditions.

Abstract: Identifying and representing object instances such as cells or nuclei is a common task in microscopy image analysis. Established machine learning workflows typically use supervised detection or segmentation followed by feature extraction or classification, which requires manual annotations and treats instance segmentation and cell representation as separate stages. We describe a new unsupervised method for cell instance segmentation and phenotypic classification from unlabeled microscopy images. Our method is based on reconstructing each image using a coarse-to-fine routing pyramid that associates pixels with spatially sparse latent sources. The resulting pixel-to-latent associations yield instance masks, while the source latents encode cell morphology. We demonstrate competitive performance in instance segmentation across diverse cell morphologies and imaging modalities, as well as generative modeling of cellular phenotypes under perturbations. Source code and checkpoints are available at https://github.com/weigertlab/routing-pyramids.

</details>


### [215] [Unlocking the Potential of Image Editing via Concept Scaling and Dense Supervision](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.16812)
*Long Cui,Xiaoqian Liu,Qi Qin,Yi Xin,Tao Lin,Jianguo Li,Linfeng Zhang*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: This paper addresses limitations in image editing frameworks by introducing a hierarchical taxonomy of over 1,000 fine-grained edit concepts, a massive 12M-pair dataset (ConceptEdit-12M), a dense supervision training strategy, and a granular evaluation benchmark (ConceptEdit-Bench), significantly outperforming prior works.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing image editing frameworks inherit training paradigms from text-to-image diffusion models, which suffer from two key discrepancies: insufficient attention to edit concept granularity and training inefficiency caused by sparse supervision signals. These limitations hinder the effectiveness and scalability of image editing models.

Method: The authors establish a comprehensive hierarchical taxonomy with over 1,000 fine-grained edit concepts and construct ConceptEdit-12M, a dataset of 12 million editing pairs synthesized via an improved framework that corrects data distribution collapse while ensuring fidelity. They also propose a dense supervision training strategy that combines multiple non-interfering concepts into single image pairs to provide richer learning signals.

Result: The proposed strategy significantly outperforms prior works in training and demonstrates enhanced training efficiency and overall model performance, validated through the ConceptEdit-Bench granular evaluation suite.

Conclusion: A library-driven approach with comprehensive concept taxonomy, massive high-quality data, and dense supervision can effectively address the granularity and efficiency gaps in image editing, setting a new standard with a dedicated benchmark for granular evaluation.

Abstract: Existing image editing frameworks predominantly follow the training paradigm of text-to-image diffusion models. However, extending this paradigm to image editing highlights two inherent discrepancies, specifically, the insufficient attention to edit concept granularity and the training inefficiency caused by sparse supervision signals. To address these issues, we establish a comprehensive hierarchical taxonomy featuring over 1,000 fine-grained edit concepts and build ConceptEdit-12M, a massive dataset of 12 million high-quality editing pairs via an improved synthesis framework. This library-driven approach effectively rectifies the distribution collapse of generated data while ensuring high data fidelity. Furthermore, we propose a dense supervision training strategy that synthesizes multiple non-interfering concepts into single image pairs. By providing richer learning signals, this strategy significantly enhances both training efficiency and overall model performance. Training results validate our strategy, significantly outperforming prior works. Finally, we present ConceptEdit-Bench, a granular evaluation suite designed to diagnose model capabilities across a vast array of real-world scenarios.

</details>


### [216] [Can Unsupervised Methods Outperform Supervised Deep Learning When Ground Truth Is Sparse? A Case Study of Bronchovascular Bundle Segmentation in Low-Dose CT](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.16855)
*Anna Mrukwa,Marek Socha,Aleksandra Suwalska,Agata Durawa,Malgorzata Jelitto,Katarzyna Dziadziuszko,Edyta Szurowska,Pawel Bozek,Michal Marczyk,Witold Rzyman,Rafal Dziadziuszko,Joanna Polanska*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: This paper presents RONALD, a bronchovascular bundle segmentation pipeline for low-dose CT (LDCT) scans that improves lung nodule detection in early-stage lung cancer by accurately segmenting vessels and bronchi, achieving near-perfect nodule retention rates (up to 100%) across two clinical datasets.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Lung cancer is often diagnosed too late due to limited radiologist availability and prolonged wait times. In very early-stage cancer, nodules are hard to detect because they are connected to or supplied by adjacent blood vessels and airway walls, so task-specific analysis of the bronchovascular bundle is crucial for efficient nodule detection.

Method: The RONALD pipeline operates on CT images and returns binary masks of vessels and bronchi in the lung parenchyma. It includes a preprocessing stage with lung, lobe, and mediastinum segmentation, followed by separate vessel and bronchial tree segmentation. The method was evaluated on the Duke Lung Cancer Screening (DLCS) dataset and the Pilot Pomeranian Lung Cancer Screening Program.

Result: The pipeline improved nodule retention compared to other segmentation methods: from 93.98% and 90.36% to 100% in the DLCS dataset, and from 83.16% and 62.36% to 99.92% in the Pomeranian dataset, while effectively segmenting the bronchovascular bundle in LDCT scans.

Conclusion: The resulting segmentations can improve lung nodule detection in the very early stages of lung cancer, demonstrating the clinical potential of bronchovascular bundle removal for lung cancer screening.

Abstract: Background
  Lung cancer remains the deadliest cancer worldwide because it is often diagnosed too late. Effective treatment depends on detection at an early screening stage. However, the growing number of patients and the limited number of radiologists lead to prolonged diagnostic waiting times. In very early stage lung cancer, nodule visibility is further reduced by adjacent blood vessels and airway walls, because nodules are often connected to or supplied by these structures. Task-specific analysis of the bronchovascular bundle is therefore important for efficient nodule detection, and its removal can increase the diagnostic potential of lung cancer screening.
  Materials and Methods
  To assess the efficacy of the proposed method, we used series from widely utilized LDCT datasets, including the Duke Lung Cancer Screening (DLCS) dataset and the Pilot Pomeranian Lung Cancer Screening Program. The proposed bronchovascular bundle segmentation pipeline, RONALD, operates on computed tomography images and returns binary masks of vessels and bronchi located in the lung parenchyma. The method includes a preprocessing stage with lung, lobe, and mediastinum segmentation, followed by separate vessel and bronchial tree segmentation.
  Results
  The proposed pipeline segmented the bronchovascular bundle in low-dose computed tomography scans while improving nodule retention compared with other segmentation methods: from 93.98% and 90.36% to 100% in DLCS, and from 83.16% and 62.36% to 99.92% in the Pomeranian dataset.
  Conclusion
  The resulting segmentations can improve lung nodule detection in the very early stages of lung cancer.

</details>


### [217] [HarnessEval-W: Agentifying the Evaluation of Visual Worlds](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.16859)
*Weiliang Chen,Haowen Sun,Jun Gao,Jiawei Chi,Hanyang Wang,Qiyu Dai,Yihao Li,Hao Li,Jingnan Gao,Yi-Hsin Hung,Xingzhuo Guo,Shangchen Miao,Zhiyuan Shi,Xiang Li,Fengrui Tian,Weihua Du,Ziqi Huang,Shenyuan Gao,Siqiao Huang,Mingyu Liu,Yifei Li,Shizun Wang,Xi Wang,Tianqi Zhang,Xue Luo,Xiyin Ren,Jinshan Ren,Xiaoyang Shen,Xiaobo Hu,Zhiyang Dou,Mingyu Ding,Yichao Yan,Xinchao Wang,Yizhou Wang,Shilong Liu,Wenzhao Zheng,Yueqi Duan,Yuan Gong,Ziwei Liu,Ming-Yu Liu,Jialong Wu,Jiangran Lyu,Fangfu Liu*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: HarnessEval-W is an agentified evaluation pipeline that brings the harness paradigm to world model benchmarking, using a hierarchical multi-agent workflow to decompose evaluation questions into measurable subproblems, gather evidence via specialized sub-agents, and produce transparent evidence trees that justify scores rather than brute-force metrics.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing world model benchmarks deliver scalar scores without reasoning chains, making their judgments untrustworthy. Humans naturally spot violations of physics, causality, and world state evolution, but no existing benchmark automates this capability—metrics are computed brute-force with no verifiable reasoning.

Method: HarnessEval-W employs a parent agent that interprets each evaluation case, decomposes the question into measurable subproblems, and spawns specialized sub-agents with tailored context and diagnostic tools. Sub-agents reason over their subproblems, the parent agent validates gathered evidence, and summarizes it into a final verdict, producing a transparent evidence tree.

Result: Applied to 18 representative world models over 330 evaluation cases. Judgments closely align with human preferences while providing verifiable, fine-grained diagnoses of every generated rollout. The full pipeline is open-sourced as a live benchmark.

Conclusion: The harness paradigm can be successfully transferred from LLM evaluation to world model benchmarking, enabling trustworthy, human-aligned, and fully verifiable evaluation through transparent reasoning chains and community-contributable growth.

Abstract: A benchmark should deliver more than a scalar score: what makes an evaluation trustworthy is the reasoning that justifies the score. This is especially critical for world models, where judging a rollout requires understanding whether physics, causality, and world state evolve correctly. Humans spot such violations naturally, yet no existing benchmark automates this capability: metrics are computed brute-force, leaving no reasoning chain that can be examined or verified. We introduce HarnessEval-W, an agentified evaluation pipeline that brings the harness paradigm from the LLM ecosystem to world model benchmarking. Rather than applying a fixed rubric, HarnessEval-W interprets the context of each evaluation case, decomposes the evaluation question into measurable subproblems, and spawns specialized sub-agents, each equipped with tailored context and diagnostic tools to reason over its own subproblem. The parent agent then validates the gathered evidence and summarizes it into the final verdict. This hierarchical workflow turns every evaluation into a transparent evidence tree whose complete reasoning chain justifies the result. We apply HarnessEval-W to 18 representative world models over 330 evaluation cases. Its judgments closely align with human preferences while providing verifiable, fine-grained diagnoses of every generated rollout. We open-source the full pipeline as a live benchmark and invite the broad community to contribute to grow new skills and evaluation cases as world models evolve.

</details>


### [218] [SplatGuide: Geometric Priors from 3D Gaussians for Pose-Free Novel View Synthesis](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.16863)
*Yejun Zhang,Zihan Wang,Xu Ji,Yihao Wang,Yuxin Hou,Junyuan Fang,Juho-Matti Kilpeläinen,Arno Solin,Hamed Rezazadegan Tavakoli,Esa Rahtu,Juho Kannala*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: SplatGuide reuses a single feed-forward 3DGS reconstruction in three complementary roles—pixel-aligned rendered images, per-Gaussian visibility voting maps for occlusion-aware reference selection, and reconstruction tokens for feature-level cross-attention—achieving state-of-the-art pose-free novel view synthesis across multiple benchmarks.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Prior pose-free novel view synthesis pipelines combining feed-forward 3DGS with multi-view diffusion extract at most one signal from the reconstruction (either pixel rendering or learned features), leaving renderable geometry, visibility cues, and learned features underutilized. This information disconnect limits the quality of synthesized views.

Method: SplatGuide reuses a single 3DGS scene in three roles: (1) rendered images provide pixel-aligned geometric conditioning; (2) per-Gaussian source-view indices are rendered into a target-view voting map for occlusion-aware reference selection; (3) reconstruction tokens supply feature-level guidance via cross-attention. All three signals are derived from the same reconstruction forward pass.

Result: SplatGuide achieves state-of-the-art pose-free novel view synthesis on RealEstate10K, DL3DV, Tanks-and-Temples, and Mip-NeRF 360. On RealEstate10K with a moderate number of input views, it surpasses the ground-truth-pose baseline.

Conclusion: Leveraging multiple complementary signals from a single 3DGS reconstruction—especially visibility-aware reference selection—closes the information disconnect in prior pipelines and yields superior pose-free novel view synthesis, even exceeding pose-conditioned baselines in certain settings.

Abstract: Generating photorealistic novel views from unposed images requires both 3D geometric understanding and the ability to synthesize unseen content. A natural strategy combines feed-forward 3DGS reconstruction with multi-view diffusion. Yet prior pipelines extract at most one signal from the reconstruction, either pixel rendering or learned features, while none exploits per-Gaussian visibility for occlusion-aware reference selection. This *information disconnect* leaves renderable geometry, visibility cues, and learned features unused. SplatGuide closes this disconnect by reusing a single 3DGS scene across three complementary roles. Rendered images provide pixel-aligned geometric conditioning. Per-Gaussian source-view indices are rendered into a target-view voting map for occlusion-aware reference selection. Reconstruction tokens supply feature-level guidance via cross-attention. All three signals derive from the same reconstruction forward pass. Across RealEstate10K, DL3DV, Tanks-and-Temples, and Mip-NeRF 360, SplatGuide achieves state-of-the-art pose-free novel view synthesis. On RealEstate10K, with a moderate number of input views, it surpasses the ground-truth-pose baseline.

</details>


### [219] [An Empirical Study of Training Pixel-Space Text-to-Image Diffusion Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.16887)
*Dengyang Jiang,Ruoyi Du,Zhennan Chen,Dongyang Liu,Zanyi Wang,Mingzhe Zheng,Xiangpeng Yang,Huanqia Cai,Aiming Hao,Yuming Jiang,Peng Gao,Harry Yang,Steven Hoi*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: This paper provides a practical recipe for training pixel-space diffusion models that rival latent-space counterparts, using a latent-to-pixel strategy that pre-trains in latent space and transitions to pixel space during post-training, achieving 3.18-4.75x inference speedups.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Pixel-space diffusion models remain under-explored at scale; existing studies focus on small-scale or class-conditional settings, and no practical recipe exists for training pixel-space models that rival well-established latent-space counterparts.

Method: The authors conduct a comprehensive empirical study showing direct large-scale pixel-space pre-training converges slowly, motivating a latent-to-pixel strategy. They systematically investigate key design choices for this transition, including weight initialization, data composition, prediction target, decoder architecture, and noise schedule.

Result: The identified recipe makes pixel-space models match or outperform latent-space counterparts while delivering 3.18 to 4.75 times end-to-end inference speedups.

Conclusion: The findings provide useful empirical insights and practical guidelines for future research on pixel-space generation.

Abstract: This paper investigates an increasingly important topic in generative modeling: pixel-space diffusion models. Although numerous studies have explored this topic, most focus on small-scale or class-conditional settings. Consequently, a practical recipe for training pixel-space models that rival or exceed well-established latent-space counterparts remains elusive. Through a comprehensive empirical study, we first observe that direct large-scale pre-training in pixel space converges substantially more slowly than in latent space. This observation motivates a latent-to-pixel strategy that acquires generative priors efficiently in latent space and transitions to pixel space during post-training. We then systematically investigate the key design choices governing this transition, including weight initialization, data composition, prediction target, decoder architecture, and noise schedule, and identify a practical recipe that makes the resulting pixel-space models match or outperform their latent-space counterparts while delivering 3.18 to 4.75 times end-to-end inference speedups. We hope that our findings provide useful empirical insights and practical guidelines for future research on pixel-space generation.

</details>
